<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q01"></a>
## Q1. First Lok Sabha opposition party and Leader of Opposition history
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → First Lok Sabha opposition party and Leader of Opposition history
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → First Lok Sabha opposition party and Leader of Opposition history  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q1 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-first-lok-sabha-opposition-party-and-leader-of-opposition-history`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. In the first Lok Sabha, the single largest party in the opposition was the Swatantra Party.
2. In the Lok Sabha, a "Leader of the Opposition" was recognised for the first time in 1969.
3. In the Lok Sabha, if a party does not have a minimum of 75 members, its leader cannot be recognised as the Leader of the Opposition.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect: the First Lok Sabha existed from 1952 to 1957, while the Swatantra Party was founded only in 1959; the Communist Party of India was the largest opposition party. Statement 2 is correct: Ram Subhag Singh was recognised as the first Leader of the Opposition in the Lok Sabha in 1969; the later Salary and Allowances of Leaders of Opposition in Parliament Act, 1977 gave the office statutory salary-and-allowance recognition. Statement 3 is incorrect: the conventional recognition threshold is one-tenth of the House, not 75 members.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**first lok sabha opposition party and leader of opposition history**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.  
**Evidence anchor:** Polity/basic/Parliament; held Series-A booklet Q1; held official-final Series-A key grid.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q02"></a>
## Q2. Desert plant leaf adaptations to inhibit water loss
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Biology and Physiology](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-biology-and-physiology.md) → Desert plant leaf adaptations to inhibit water loss
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science Biology and Physiology → Desert plant leaf adaptations to inhibit water loss  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q2 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-desert-plant-leaf-adaptations-to-inhibit-water-loss`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following leaf modifications occur(s) in the desert areas to inhibit water loss?
1. Hard and waxy leaves
2. Tiny leaves
3. Thorns instead of leaves
Select the correct answer using the code given below:

(a) 2 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

All three listed modifications are crucial adaptations in desert plants, commonly known as xerophytes, to minimize water loss and survive in arid environments. Hard and waxy leaves: A thick, waxy cuticle on the leaf surface forms a protective layer that significantly reduces the rate of transpiration, the process by which water vapor escapes from plant leaves into the atmosphere. The waxy coating acts as a barrier, making the leaf surface less permeable to water. Hardness can also provide structural integrity, preventing wilting under water stress. Tiny leaves: Reducing the size of leaves directly decreases the total surface area exposed to the dry, hot desert air and intense sunlight. A smaller surface area means less area for stomata (pores for gas exchange and transpiration), thereby lowering the rate of water evaporation and transpiration from the leaf surface. In many desert plants, leaves are significantly reduced or even scale-like. Thorns instead of leaves: In extreme cases, such as in cacti and some euphorbias, leaves are modified into sharp thorns. This adaptation drastically reduces the surface area available for transpiration, thereby conserving water to a great extent. The stem often takes over the photosynthetic function. Additionally, thorns provide a defense mechanism against herbivores that might otherwise consume the water-storing tissues of the plant. Since all three modifications effectively contribute to inhibiting water loss in desert environments, option D, which includes all three statements, is the correct answer.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1, 3.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1, 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**desert plant leaf adaptations to inhibit water loss**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q03"></a>
## Q3. NSSO 70th Round Agricultural Household Survey data
**Primary route:** Economy → [Land Reforms, Green Revolution and Cropping Systems](../../topics/economy/land-reforms-green-revolution-and-cropping-systems.md) → NSSO 70th Round Agricultural Household Survey data
**Primary classification:** Economy → Land Reforms Green Revolution and Cropping Systems → NSSO 70th Round Agricultural Household Survey data  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q3 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/11_Land-Reforms-Green-Revolution-and-Cropping-Systems](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/11_Land-Reforms-Green-Revolution-and-Cropping-Systems.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-nsso-70th-round-agricultural-household-survey-data`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

As per the NSSO 70th Round "Situation Assessment Survey of Agricultural Households", consider the following statements:
1. Rajasthan has the highest percentage share of agricultural households among its rural households.
2. Out of the total agricultural households in the country, a little over 60 percent belong to OBCS.
3. In Kerala, a little over 60 percent of agricultural households reported to have received maximum income from sources other than agricultural activities.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 2 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question pertains to the findings of the NSSO 70th Round "Situation Assessment Survey of Agricultural Households" conducted in 2012-13. Statement 1 — This statement is correct. According to the NSSO 70th Round report, Rajasthan indeed had the highest percentage of agricultural households (78.4%) among its rural households in the country. Statement 2 — This statement is incorrect. As per the NSSO 70th Round data, the distribution of agricultural households by social group showed that OBCs constituted 45.3% of the total agricultural households, not 'a little over 60%'. The remaining percentages were Scheduled Tribes (14.7%), Scheduled Castes (13.9%), and Others (26.1%). Statement 3 — This statement is correct. The survey revealed that in Kerala, 61.2% of agricultural households reported 'other sources' (primarily wage/salary employment and non-agricultural business) as their principal source of income, which is 'a little over 60%'. This highlights the state's significant reliance on non-farm income even within agricultural households.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2; omits supported statement(s) 1, 3.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**nsso 70th round agricultural household survey data**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q04"></a>
## Q4. National Green Tribunal versus Central Pollution Control Board
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Environmental Impact Assessment and NGT](../../topics/environment/environmental-impact-assessment-and-ngt.md) → National Green Tribunal versus Central Pollution Control Board
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Environmental Impact Assessment and NGT → National Green Tribunal versus Central Pollution Control Board  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q4 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/16_Environmental-Impact-Assessment-and-NGT](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/16_Environmental-Impact-Assessment-and-NGT.md)  
**Secondary owner:** [Polity/basic/Statutory-Regulatory-and-Quasi-Judicial-Bodies](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Statutory-Regulatory-and-Quasi-Judicial-Bodies.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-national-green-tribunal-versus-central-pollution-control-board`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

How is the National Green Tribunal (NGT) different from the Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB)?
1. The NGT has been established by an Act whereas the CPCB has been created by an executive order of the Government.
2. The NGT provides environmental justice and helps reduce the burden of litigation in the higher courts whereas the CPCB promotes cleanliness of streams and wells, and aims to improve the quality of air in the country.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The National Green Tribunal (NGT) was indeed established by an Act of Parliament, specifically the National Green Tribunal Act, 2010. However, the Central Pollution Control Board (CPCB) was also established under an Act of Parliament, the Water (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1974. It was later also entrusted with powers and functions under the Air (Prevention and Control of Pollution) Act, 1981. CPCB is a statutory body, not created by an executive order.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**national green tribunal versus central pollution control board**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q05"></a>
## Q5. Ninth Schedule laws and judicial review in Constitution
**Primary route:** Polity → [Amendment of the Constitution & Basic Structure](../../topics/polity/amendment-and-basic-structure.md) → Ninth Schedule laws and judicial review in Constitution
**Primary classification:** Polity → Amendment and Basic Structure → Ninth Schedule laws and judicial review in Constitution  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q5 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Amendment-and-Basic-Structure](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Amendment-and-Basic-Structure.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-ninth-schedule-laws-and-judicial-review-in-constitution`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Parliament of India can place a particular law in the Ninth Schedule of the Constitution of India.
2. The validity of a law placed in the Ninth Schedule cannot be examined by any court and no judgement can be made on it.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. The Parliament of India has the power to amend the Constitution, and this power includes adding entries to the Ninth Schedule. The Ninth Schedule was added by the 1st Constitutional Amendment Act, 1951, to protect certain laws, especially those related to land reforms, from being challenged in courts on the ground of violating fundamental rights. Since then, Parliament has continued to add various laws to this Schedule through subsequent constitutional amendments.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q06"></a>
## Q6. Merchant Discount Rate meaning in digital payments context
**Primary route:** Economy → [Services, Digital Economy, Fintech and Platform Markets](../../topics/economy/services-digital-economy-fintech-and-platform-markets.md) → Merchant Discount Rate meaning in digital payments context
**Primary classification:** Economy → Services Digital Economy Fintech and Platform Markets → Merchant Discount Rate meaning in digital payments context  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q6 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: single-best answer · factual discrimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/24_Services-Digital-Economy-Fintech-and-Platform-Markets](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/24_Services-Digital-Economy-Fintech-and-Platform-Markets.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-merchant-discount-rate-meaning-in-digital-payments-context`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following best describes the term "Merchant Discount Rate" sometimes seen in news?

(a) The incentive given by a bank to a merchant for accepting payments through debit cards pertaining to that bank.  
(b) The amount paid back by banks to their customers when they use debit cards for financial transactions for purchasing goods or services.  
(c) The charge to a merchant by a bank for accepting payments from his customers through the bank's debit cards.  
(d) The incentive given by the Government to merchants for promoting digital payments by their customers through Point of Sale (PoS) machines and debit cards.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — The charge to a merchant by a bank for accepting payments from his customers through the bank's debit cards.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Merchant Discount Rate (MDR) is the fee charged to a merchant by a bank for providing payment services, specifically for accepting payments from customers via debit cards, credit cards, or other digital payment instruments. This charge is typically a percentage of the transaction value. The merchant pays this rate to the acquiring bank (the bank that provides the PoS terminal or payment gateway service to the merchant) for processing the transaction. Option A describes an 'incentive' from a bank to a merchant, which is incorrect as MDR is a cost, not an incentive. Option B describes 'cashback' or a similar return to customers, which is fundamentally different from MDR. Option D describes an 'incentive given by the Government', which, while relevant to digital payments promotion (e.g., zero MDR for RuPay/UPI, with government bearing the cost), does not define MDR itself; rather, it describes a government policy related to it. Option C accurately defines MDR as a charge levied on the merchant for accepting digital payments.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: The incentive given by a bank to a merchant for accepting payments through debit cards pertaining to that bank.** — Incorrect: it identifies **The incentive given by a bank to a merchant for accepting payments through debit cards pertaining to that bank.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The charge to a merchant by a bank for accepting payments from his customers through the bank's debit cards.**.
- **B: The amount paid back by banks to their customers when they use debit cards for financial transactions for purchasing goods or services.** — Incorrect: it identifies **The amount paid back by banks to their customers when they use debit cards for financial transactions for purchasing goods or services.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The charge to a merchant by a bank for accepting payments from his customers through the bank's debit cards.**.
- **C: The charge to a merchant by a bank for accepting payments from his customers through the bank's debit cards.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: The incentive given by the Government to merchants for promoting digital payments by their customers through Point of Sale (PoS) machines and debit cards.** — Incorrect: it identifies **The incentive given by the Government to merchants for promoting digital payments by their customers through Point of Sale (PoS) machines and debit cards.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The charge to a merchant by a bank for accepting payments from his customers through the bank's debit cards.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**merchant discount rate meaning in digital payments context**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

## Q7. Nuclear Suppliers Group membership consequences for a country
**Primary route:** International Relations → [Regional, Global and Minilateral Groupings](../../topics/international-relations/regional-global-and-minilateral-groupings.md) → Nuclear Suppliers Group membership consequences for a country
**Primary classification:** International Relations → Regional Global and Minilateral Groupings → Nuclear Suppliers Group membership consequences for a country  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q7 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [International-Relations/basic/10_Regional-Global-and-Minilateral-Groupings](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/International-Relations/basic/10_Regional-Global-and-Minilateral-Groupings.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `international-relations-nuclear-suppliers-group-membership-consequences-for-a-country`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is/are the consequence/consequences of a country becoming the member of the 'Nuclear Suppliers Group'?
1. It will have access to the latest and most efficient nuclear technologies.
2. It automatically becomes a member of "The Treaty on the Non-Proliferation of Nuclear Weapons (NPT)".
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. The Nuclear Suppliers Group (NSG) is a group of nuclear supplier countries that seeks to prevent nuclear proliferation by controlling the export of materials, equipment, and technology that could be used to manufacture nuclear weapons. While its primary aim is non-proliferation, membership in the NSG facilitates legitimate trade and cooperation in the civilian nuclear sector among member states. Member countries, by adhering to strict export controls and international safeguards, gain greater trust and open doors for access to advanced nuclear technologies for peaceful applications, such as power generation, medicine, and agriculture. This access is crucial for countries looking to expand their civilian nuclear programs efficiently and safely.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**nuclear suppliers group membership consequences for a country**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q8. Equalization tax on non-resident online advertising services India
**Primary route:** Economy → [Taxation, GST, Finance Commission and Fiscal Federalism](../../topics/economy/taxation-gst-finance-commission-and-fiscal-federalism.md) → Equalization tax on non-resident online advertising services India
**Primary classification:** Economy → Taxation GST Finance Commission and Fiscal Federalism → Equalization tax on non-resident online advertising services India

**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

Statement 1 is incorrect. The Equalization Levy (often termed 'Google Tax') was introduced in India by the Finance Act, 2016, as a separate chapter (Chapter VIII) within the Finance Act itself, and not as a part of the Income Tax Act, 1961. This distinct legal framework was chosen to ensure that the levy could operate independently of the traditional income tax regime and its associated rules.

## Q12. Aadhaar card as citizenship proof and deactivation rules
**Primary route:** Governance → [Digital Public Infrastructure and Data Governance](../../topics/governance/digital-public-infrastructure-and-data-governance.md) → Aadhaar card as citizenship proof and deactivation rules
**Primary classification:** Governance → Digital Public Infrastructure and Data Governance → Aadhaar card as citizenship proof and deactivation rules  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q12 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Governance/basic/06_Digital-Public-Infrastructure-and-Data-Governance](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Governance/basic/06_Digital-Public-Infrastructure-and-Data-Governance.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `governance-aadhaar-card-as-citizenship-proof-and-deactivation-rules`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. Aadhaar card can be used as a proof of citizenship or domicile.
2. Once issued, Aadhaar number cannot be deactivated or omitted by the Issuing Authority.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The Unique Identification Authority of India (UIDAI) and the Aadhaar Act, 2016, explicitly state that an Aadhaar card is not a proof of citizenship or domicile. Section 9 of the Aadhaar (Targeted Delivery of Financial and Other Subsidies, Benefits and Services) Act, 2016, clearly states: "The Aadhaar number or the authentication thereof shall not, by itself, confer any right of citizenship or domicile in respect of an Aadhaar number holder." Aadhaar is primarily an identity proof for residents of India, regardless of their nationality.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: its combination does not match the verified truth-set.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**aadhaar card as citizenship proof and deactivation rules**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q13"></a>
## Q13. Aral Sea Lake Baikal shrinkage due to human activities
**Primary route:** Geography → [Lakes: Formation & Types](../../topics/geography/lakes.md) → Aral Sea Lake Baikal shrinkage due to human activities
**Primary classification:** Geography → Lakes → Aral Sea Lake Baikal shrinkage due to human activities  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q13 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/09_Lakes](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/09_Lakes.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-aral-sea-lake-baikal-shrinkage-due-to-human-activities`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Aral Sea
2. Black Sea
3. Lake Baikal
Select the correct answer using the code given below: Which of the following has/have shrunk immensely/dried up in the recent past due to human activities?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3  
(c) 2 only  
(d) 1 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Aral Sea is a well-documented example of a large inland lake that has shrunk immensely and largely dried up due to human activities. This environmental disaster, primarily occurring from the 1960s onwards, was caused by the diversion of the Amu Darya and Syr Darya rivers (its main water sources) for large-scale irrigation projects, particularly for cotton farming, by the Soviet Union. The Black Sea, while facing significant environmental issues such as pollution (eutrophication, industrial waste, marine litter), overfishing, and habitat degradation, has not 'shrunk immensely' or 'dried up'. Its volume has been impacted by anoxia in deeper waters, leading to a loss of habitable volume, but not a reduction in its overall surface area or complete desiccation.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

## Q14. Organization that releases Rule of Law Index annually
**Primary route:** Governance → [Good Governance Concepts and Frameworks](../../topics/governance/good-governance-concepts-and-frameworks.md) → Organization that releases Rule of Law Index annually
**Primary classification:** Governance → Good Governance Concepts and Frameworks → Organization that releases Rule of Law Index annually  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q14 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Governance/basic/01_Good-Governance-Concepts-and-Frameworks](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Governance/basic/01_Good-Governance-Concepts-and-Frameworks.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `governance-organization-that-releases-rule-of-law-index-annually`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

"Rule of Law Index" is released by which of the following?

(a) Amnesty International  
(b) International Court of Justice  
(c) The Office of UN Commissioner for Human Rights  
(d) World Justice Project  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — World Justice Project**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is (D) World Justice Project. The 'Rule of Law Index' is an annual report published by the World Justice Project (WJP), an independent, multidisciplinary organization dedicated to advancing the rule of law worldwide. The Index measures how the rule of law is experienced by the general public in various countries, based on eight factors: Constraints on Government Powers, Absence of Corruption, Open Government, Fundamental Rights, Order and Security, Regulatory Enforcement, Civil Justice, and Criminal Justice. Amnesty International (A) is a global non-governmental organization focused on human rights, publishing reports like 'The State of the World's Human Rights'. The International Court of Justice (B) is the principal judicial organ of the United Nations, responsible for settling legal disputes between states and providing advisory opinions. The Office of UN Commissioner for Human Rights (C) (OHCHR) is the leading UN entity on human rights, working to promote and protect human rights globally. Neither Amnesty International, the International Court of Justice, nor the OHCHR release the 'Rule of Law Index'.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Amnesty International** — Incorrect: it identifies **Amnesty International**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **World Justice Project**.
- **B: International Court of Justice** — Incorrect: it identifies **International Court of Justice**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **World Justice Project**.
- **C: The Office of UN Commissioner for Human Rights** — Incorrect: it identifies **The Office of UN Commissioner for Human Rights**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **World Justice Project**.
- **D: World Justice Project** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**organization that releases rule of law index annually**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q15"></a>
## Q15. Institution linking all ATMs across India
**Primary route:** Economy → [Services, Digital Economy, Fintech and Platform Markets](../../topics/economy/services-digital-economy-fintech-and-platform-markets.md) → Institution linking all ATMs across India
**Primary classification:** Economy → Services Digital Economy Fintech and Platform Markets → Institution linking all ATMs across India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q15 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: single-best answer · factual discrimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/24_Services-Digital-Economy-Fintech-and-Platform-Markets](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/24_Services-Digital-Economy-Fintech-and-Platform-Markets.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-institution-linking-all-atms-across-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following links all the ATMs in India?

(a) Indian Banks' Association  
(b) National Securities Depository Limited  
(c) National Payments Corporation of India  
(d) Reserve Bank of India  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — National Payments Corporation of India**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is National Payments Corporation of India (NPCI). NPCI is an umbrella organisation for operating retail payments and settlement systems in India. It was authorized by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) to take over the operations of the National Financial Switch (NFS). NFS is the largest network of shared Automated Teller Machines (ATMs) in India, designed to facilitate interoperability and link all ATMs across the country. Option A, Indian Banks' Association (IBA), is an unregistered, voluntary association and a representative body of management of banking in India. It focuses on strengthening and coordinating the banking industry rather than operating payment infrastructure like ATM networks. Option B, National Securities Depository Limited (NSDL), is a central securities depository in India. Its primary function is to hold securities in dematerialized form and facilitate their transfer and settlement. It is not involved in the operation of ATM networks.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Indian Banks' Association** — Incorrect: it identifies **Indian Banks' Association**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **National Payments Corporation of India**.
- **B: National Securities Depository Limited** — Incorrect: it identifies **National Securities Depository Limited**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **National Payments Corporation of India**.
- **C: National Payments Corporation of India** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: Reserve Bank of India** — Incorrect: it identifies **Reserve Bank of India**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **National Payments Corporation of India**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**institution linking all atms across india**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q16"></a>
## Q16. Capital Adequacy Ratio banking regulation and determination
**Primary route:** Economy → [Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation](../../topics/economy/banking-structure-nbfcs-and-financial-regulation.md) → Capital Adequacy Ratio banking regulation and determination
**Primary classification:** Economy → NPAs Basel Norms Resolution and Financial Inclusion → Capital Adequacy Ratio banking regulation and determination  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q16 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/06_NPAs-Basel-Norms-Resolution-and-Financial-Inclusion](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/06_NPAs-Basel-Norms-Resolution-and-Financial-Inclusion.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-capital-adequacy-ratio-banking-regulation-and-determination`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) is the amount that banks have to maintain in the form of their own funds to offset any loss that banks incur if the account-holders fail to repay dues.
2. CAR is decided by each individual bank.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR), also known as Capital to Risk-weighted Assets Ratio (CRAR), is a key indicator of a bank's financial strength and stability. It represents the ratio of a bank's capital to its risk-weighted assets. The primary purpose of CAR is to ensure that banks have enough capital to absorb potential losses from various risks, including credit risk (i.e., when account-holders or borrowers fail to repay their dues), operational risk, and market risk. This capital acts as a buffer, protecting depositors and promoting confidence in the banking system.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

## Q17. Aadhaar Open APIs electronic integration and biometric authentication
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Digital India, India Stack and Digital Infrastructure](../../topics/science-and-technology/digital-india-india-stack-and-digital-infrastructure.md) → Aadhaar Open APIs electronic integration and biometric authentication
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Digital India and India Stack UPI Aadhaar → Aadhaar Open APIs electronic integration and biometric authentication  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q17 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/08_Digital-India-and-India-Stack-UPI-Aadhaar](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/08_Digital-India-and-India-Stack-UPI-Aadhaar.md)  
**Secondary owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/25_Computing-Fundamentals-Hardware-Software-Networks-and-Cloud](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/25_Computing-Fundamentals-Hardware-Software-Networks-and-Cloud.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-aadhaar-open-apis-electronic-integration-and-biometric-authentication`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The identity platform 'Aadhaar' provides open "Application Programming Interfaces (APIs)". What does it imply?
1. It can be integrated into any electronic device.
2. Online authentication using iris is possible.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1: The term 'open Application Programming Interfaces (APIs)' implies that the functionalities of a system, in this case, Aadhaar, are made available for external developers and other software systems to access and integrate. This allows for interoperability and enables Aadhaar-based services to be built into a wide range of electronic devices, such as smartphones, point-of-sale (PoS) terminals, attendance systems, and various other applications. statement 1 is correct. Statement 2: Aadhaar supports multiple authentication modes, including demographic, One-Time Password (OTP), and biometric (fingerprint and iris). The UIDAI provides APIs for these authentication services. Online authentication using iris scan is indeed a supported and widely used biometric method for Aadhaar authentication. The Aadhaar ecosystem includes provisions for capturing and verifying iris biometrics online through its APIs. statement 2 is correct. Since both statements are accurate implications of Aadhaar providing open APIs, the correct option is C.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: its combination does not match the verified truth-set.

(b) Morocco and Tunisia

This conflict led to widespread displacement, destruction of infrastructure, and blockades, resulting in a severe humanitarian crisis. Millions faced acute food insecurity, and widespread famine was declared or threatened in various parts of the country, directly attributable to the ongoing war and related disruptions.

- **C: Venezuela and Colombia** — Incorrect: it identifies **Venezuela and Colombia**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Yemen and South Sudan**.
- **D: Yemen and South Sudan** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q19"></a>
## Q19. Wood's Dispatch provisions on education in colonial India
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Macaulay’s Minute and Colonial Education](../../topics/modern-history/macaulays-minute-colonial-education.md) → Wood's Dispatch provisions on education in colonial India
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Social and Cultural Policy Education Press → Wood's Dispatch provisions on education in colonial India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q19 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/09_Social-and-Cultural-Policy-Education-Press](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/09_Social-and-Cultural-Policy-Education-Press.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-wood-s-dispatch-provisions-on-education-in-colonial-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Grants-in-Aid system was introduced.
2. Establishment of universities was recommended.
3. English as a medium of instruction at all levels of education was recommended.
Select the correct answer using the code given below: Regarding Wood's Dispatch, which of the following statements are true?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only.  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Wood's Dispatch of 1854 was a significant milestone in the history of education in British India. Let's analyze each statement: Grants-in-Aid system was introduced: This statement is true . The Dispatch explicitly recommended a system of grants-in-aid to encourage private enterprise in education. This was a crucial step towards expanding educational facilities beyond government initiatives. Establishment of universities was recommended: This statement is true . The Dispatch recommended the establishment of universities in Calcutta, Bombay, and Madras, modelled on the University of London. These universities were to be examining bodies, conferring degrees, and were subsequently established in 1857. English as a medium of instruction at all levels of education was recommended: This statement is false . While Wood's Dispatch promoted English as the medium of instruction for higher education (collegiate level), it did not recommend it for all levels. Instead, it advocated for vernacular languages at the primary level and Anglo-vernacular (a mix of English and vernaculars) at the high school level. The goal was to promote European knowledge through English in higher education, but also to develop vernaculars for mass education. Based on this analysis, only statements 1 and 2 are correct. the correct option is A.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 2 and 3 only.** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**wood's dispatch provisions on education in colonial india**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q20. Parliamentary Committee scrutinizing delegated legislation India
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → Parliamentary Committee scrutinizing delegated legislation India
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → Parliamentary Committee scrutinizing delegated legislation India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q20 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-parliamentary-committee-scrutinizing-delegated-legislation-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the Parliament of India, which of the following Parliamentary Committees scrutinizes and reports to the House whether the powers to make regulations, rules, sub-rules, by-laws, etc. conferred by the Constitution or delegated by the Parliament are being properly exercised by the Executive within the scope of such delegation?

(a) Committee on Government Assurances  
(b) Committee on Subordinate Legislation  
(c) Rules Committee  
(d) Business Advisory Committee  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Committee on Subordinate Legislation**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question specifically asks for the Parliamentary Committee that scrutinizes whether the executive is properly exercising its delegated powers to make rules, regulations, and by-laws. This function precisely describes the role of the Committee on Subordinate Legislation. Option B: Committee on Subordinate Legislation is the correct answer. This committee examines and reports to the House whether the powers to make regulations, rules, sub-rules, and by-laws, conferred by the Constitution or delegated by Parliament, are being properly exercised by the Executive within the scope of such delegation. It was constituted in 1953 and has 15 members. Option A: Committee on Government Assurances scrutinizes the assurances, promises, undertakings, etc., given by ministers on the floor of the House and reports on the extent to which they have been implemented. Option C: Rules Committee considers matters of procedure and conduct of business in the House and recommends necessary amendments or additions to the rules of the House.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Committee on Government Assurances** — Incorrect: it identifies **Committee on Government Assurances**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Committee on Subordinate Legislation**.
- **B: Committee on Subordinate Legislation** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: Rules Committee** — Incorrect: it identifies **Rules Committee**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Committee on Subordinate Legislation**.
- **D: Business Advisory Committee** — Incorrect: it identifies **Business Advisory Committee**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Committee on Subordinate Legislation**.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q21"></a>
## Q21. Right to Education Act teacher qualification eligibility provisions
**Primary route:** Social Justice → [Education and Human Resource Development](../../topics/social-justice/education-and-human-resource-development.md) → Right to Education Act teacher qualification eligibility provisions
**Primary classification:** Social Justice → Education and Human Resource Development → Right to Education Act teacher qualification eligibility provisions  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q21 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Social-Justice/basic/04_Education-and-Human-Resource-Development](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Social-Justice/basic/04_Education-and-Human-Resource-Development.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `social-justice-right-to-education-act-teacher-qualification-eligibility-provisions`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. As per the Right to Education (RTE) Act, to be eligible for appointment as a teacher in a State, a person would be required to possess the minimum qualification laid down by the concerned State Council of Teacher Education.
2. As per the RTE Act, for teaching primary classes, a candidate is required to pass a Teacher Eligibility Test conducted in accordance with the National Council of Teacher Education guidelines.
3. In India, more than 90% of teacher education institutions are directly under the State Governments.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

This statement is incorrect. Section 23(1) of the Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education (RTE) Act, 2009, clearly states that the minimum qualifications for appointment as a teacher shall be laid down by an 'academic authority, authorised by the Central Government'. The National Council for Teacher Education (NCTE) is the academic authority authorized by the Central Government for this purpose, not a State Council of Teacher Education. This statement is correct. The NCTE, acting as the academic authority under the RTE Act, issued a notification on August 23, 2010, specifying that one of the essential qualifications for appointment as a teacher in classes I-VIII (which includes primary classes) is passing the Teacher Eligibility Test (TET) to be conducted by the appropriate Government in accordance with the guidelines framed by the NCTE. This provision is directly derived from the powers granted by the RTE Act.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 1 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**right to education act teacher qualification eligibility provisions**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Crafts, Textiles, Folk and Tribal Traditions](../../topics/art-and-culture/crafts-textiles-folk-and-tribal-traditions.md) → Chapchar Kut Khongjom Parba Thang-Ta folk cultural traditions
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Crafts Textiles Folk and Tribal Traditions → Chapchar Kut Khongjom Parba Thang-Ta folk cultural traditions  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q22 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: pair matching · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-chapchar-kut-khongjom-parba-thang-ta-folk-cultural-traditions`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs: (Tradition — State)
1. Chapchar Kut festival — Mizoram
2. Khongjom Parba ballad — Manipur
3. Thang-Ta dance — Sikkim Which of the pairs given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's evaluate each pair: Chapchar Kut festival | Mizoram: This pair is correct. Chapchar Kut is one of the most important and popular festivals of Mizoram. It is a spring festival celebrated after the 'jhum' (shifting cultivation) operations, specifically after the jungle has been cut and left to dry, before burning. The famous 'Cheraw' (bamboo dance) is performed during this festival. Khongjom Parba ballad | Manipur: This pair is correct. Khongjom Parba is a traditional Manipuri ballad or narrative song form that recounts the events of the Anglo-Manipur War of 1891, particularly the Battle of Khongjom. It is a significant part of Manipur's oral historical tradition, often performed with a Pena (a traditional string instrument).

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

- **B: 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1, 2.
- **D: 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.

## Q23. Food Safety and Standards Act 2006 and FSSAI structure
**Primary route:** Governance → [Regulatory Governance and Independent Regulators](../../topics/governance/regulatory-governance-and-independent-regulators.md) → Food Safety and Standards Act 2006 and FSSAI structure
**Primary classification:** Governance → Regulatory Governance and Independent Regulators → Food Safety and Standards Act 2006 and FSSAI structure

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Food Safety and Standards Act, 2006 replaced the Prevention of Food Adulteration Act, 1954.
2. The Food Safety and Standards Authority of India (FSSAI) is under the charge of Director General of Health Services in the Union Ministry of Health and Family Welfare.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. The Food Safety and Standards Act (FSSA), 2006 was enacted to consolidate various laws relating to food and to establish the Food Safety and Standards Authority of India (FSSAI). It specifically repealed several older acts and orders, including the prominent Prevention of Food Adulteration Act, 1954, the Fruit Products Order, 1955, and the Meat Food Products Order, 1973, among others. The intent was to move from a multi-layered, fragmented regulatory system to a single, comprehensive law for food safety and standards in India.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q24"></a>
## Q24. Two-state solution in international affairs context
**Primary route:** International Relations → [West Asia, Energy Security and Connectivity](../../topics/international-relations/west-asia-energy-security-and-connectivity.md) → Two-state solution in international affairs context
**Primary classification:** International Relations → West Asia Energy Security and Connectivity → Two-state solution in international affairs context  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q24 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [International-Relations/basic/06_West-Asia-Energy-Security-and-Connectivity](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/International-Relations/basic/06_West-Asia-Energy-Security-and-Connectivity.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `international-relations-two-state-solution-in-international-affairs-context`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The term "two-state solution" is sometimes mentioned in the news in the context of the affairs of

(a) China  
(b) Israel  
(c) Iraq  
(d) Yemen  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Israel**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The term "two-state solution" refers to a proposed framework for resolving the Israeli,Palestinian conflict. This solution envisions two states for two groups of people: an independent State of Palestine alongside the State of Israel. The goal is for these two states to coexist peacefully and securely. This concept has been the dominant framework for international diplomatic efforts to resolve the conflict for several decades, supported by the United Nations and many countries globally. its mention in the news is almost exclusively in the context of the affairs of Israel and the Palestinian territories.

**Option-by-option check:**

- **C: Iraq** — Incorrect: it identifies **Iraq**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Israel**.
- **D: Yemen** — Incorrect: it identifies **Yemen**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Israel**.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q25"></a>
## Q25. National Food Security Act 2013 beneficiary categories and provisions
**Primary route:** Social Justice → [Food and Nutrition Security](../../topics/social-justice/food-and-nutrition-security.md) → National Food Security Act 2013 beneficiary categories and provisions
**Primary classification:** Social Justice → Poverty Hunger Food and Nutrition Security → National Food Security Act 2013 beneficiary categories and provisions  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q25 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Social-Justice/basic/02_Poverty-Hunger-Food-and-Nutrition-Security](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Social-Justice/basic/02_Poverty-Hunger-Food-and-Nutrition-Security.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `social-justice-national-food-security-act-2013-beneficiary-categories-and-provisions`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the provisions made under the National Food Security Act, 2013, consider the following statements:
1. The families coming under the category of 'below poverty line (BPL)' only are eligible to receive subsidised food grains.
2. The eldest woman in a household, of age 18 years or above, shall be the head of the household for the purpose of issuance of a ration card.
3. Pregnant women and lactating mothers are entitled to a 'take-home ration' of 1600 calories per day during pregnancy and for six months thereafter.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The National Food Security Act (NFSA), 2013, aims to provide food and nutritional security by ensuring access to adequate quantities of quality food at affordable prices. It covers up to 75% of the rural population and 50% of the urban population to receive subsidized food grains under the Targeted Public Distribution System (TPDS). This coverage extends beyond just 'Below Poverty Line (BPL)' families, encompassing Antyodaya Anna Yojana (AAY) households (the poorest of the poor) and priority households, identified by the State/UT governments based on specific criteria. the word 'only' makes this statement false. Statement 2 is correct. A key provision of the NFSA, 2013, is the empowerment of women. Section 13 of the Act mandates that the eldest woman in a household, who is 18 years of age or above, shall be the head of the household for the purpose of issuance of a ration card. This provision promotes gender equity and control over resources within the family.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 1 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q26"></a>
## Q26. Geographical Indications of Goods Act 1999 treaty compliance
**Primary route:** Economy → [Foreign Trade, WTO, FTAs and Protectionism](../../topics/economy/foreign-trade-wto-ftas-and-protectionism.md) → Geographical Indications of Goods Act 1999 treaty compliance
**Primary classification:** Economy → Foreign Trade WTO FTAs and Protectionism → Geographical Indications of Goods Act 1999 treaty compliance  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q26 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/20_Foreign-Trade-WTO-FTAs-and-Protectionism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-geographical-indications-of-goods-act-1999-treaty-compliance`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

India enacted The Geographical Indications of Goods (Registration and Protection) Act, 1999 in order to comply with the obligations to

(a) ILO  
(b) IMF  
(c) UNCTAD  
(d) WTO  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — WTO**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is WTO. India enacted The Geographical Indications of Goods (Registration and Protection) Act, 1999, specifically to comply with its obligations under the Agreement on Trade-Related Aspects of Intellectual Property Rights (TRIPS Agreement), which is administered by the World Trade Organization (WTO). The TRIPS Agreement, signed in 1994, is a multilateral agreement on intellectual property rights that includes provisions for the protection of Geographical Indications (GIs). This act provides for the registration and better protection of geographical indications relating to goods in India. ILO (International Labour Organization) deals with labour standards, workers' rights, and social protection; it has no direct mandate over intellectual property rights like GIs. IMF (International Monetary Fund) focuses on global monetary cooperation, financial stability, and facilitating international trade; it does not deal with intellectual property protection. UNCTAD (United Nations Conference on Trade and Development)

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: ILO** — Incorrect: it identifies **ILO**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **WTO**.
- **B: IMF** — Incorrect: it identifies **IMF**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **WTO**.
- **C: UNCTAD** — Incorrect: it identifies **UNCTAD**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **WTO**.
- **D: WTO** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**geographical indications of goods act 1999 treaty compliance**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q27. State government mineral mining rights and distribution India
**Primary route:** Geography → [Mineral & Energy Resources: Classification, World Belts & Strategic Significance](../../topics/geography/mineral-energy-resources-world-and-india.md) → State government mineral mining rights and distribution India
**Primary classification:** Geography → Mineral Energy Resources World and India → State government mineral mining rights and distribution India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q27 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/31_Mineral-Energy-Resources-World-and-India](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/31_Mineral-Energy-Resources-World-and-India.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-state-government-mineral-mining-rights-and-distribution-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. In India, State Governments do not have the power to auction non-coal mines.
2. Andhra Pradesh and Jharkhand do not have gold mines.
3. Rajasthan has iron ore mines.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's evaluate each statement: This statement is incorrect. The Mines and Minerals (Development and Regulation) Act, 1957, was significantly amended in 2015. One of the key reforms introduced by the MMDR Amendment Act, 2015, was to mandate the auction of mineral concessions (prospecting license-cum-mining lease and mining lease) for all major minerals, including non-coal minerals. As per the amended act, State Governments are empowered to conduct the auction of these mineral blocks. This statement is incorrect. Both Andhra Pradesh and Jharkhand have reported gold mines and reserves. In Andhra Pradesh, areas like Jonnagiri (Kurnool district) and Anantapur North have gold deposits and mining projects. In Jharkhand, gold reserves have been found in districts like East Singhbhum, and notable mines include Lava gold mine in Chandil and Parasi in Ranchi district, which is considered one of the largest gold reserves discovered in India.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 2; omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2; omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **C: 1 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.
- **D: 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

## Q29. Indian cities on longitude closest to Delhi
**Primary route:** Geography → [Political Geography: Boundaries, Frontiers & Geopolitical Theories](../../topics/geography/indian-political-geography-boundaries-and-neighbours.md) → Indian cities on longitude closest to Delhi
**Primary classification:** Geography → Indian Political Geography Boundaries and Neighbours → Indian cities on longitude closest to Delhi  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q29 · Difficulty: Easy · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/35_Indian-Political-Geography-Boundaries-and-Neighbours](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/35_Indian-Political-Geography-Boundaries-and-Neighbours.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-indian-cities-on-longitude-closest-to-delhi`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Among the following cities, which one lies on a longitude closest to that of Delhi ?

(a) Bengaluru  
(b) Hyderabad  
(c) Nagpur  
(d) Pune  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Bengaluru**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

To determine which city lies on a longitude closest to that of Delhi, we need to compare their respective longitudes: The approximate longitude of Delhi is 77.21° East. The approximate longitude of Bengaluru is 77.58° East. Difference from Delhi: |77.58° E - 77.21° E| = 0.37° The approximate longitude of Hyderabad is 78.47° East. Difference from Delhi: |78.47° E - 77.21° E| = 1.26° The approximate longitude of Nagpur is 79.08° East. Difference from Delhi: |79.08° E - 77.21° E| = 1.87° The approximate longitude of Pune is 73.85° East. Difference from Delhi: |73.85° E - 77.21° E| = |-3.36°| = 3.36° Comparing the absolute differences, Bengaluru (0.37°) has the smallest difference in longitude from Delhi. Bengaluru lies on a longitude closest to that of Delhi.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Bengaluru** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: Hyderabad** — Incorrect: it identifies **Hyderabad**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Bengaluru**.
- **C: Nagpur** — Incorrect: it identifies **Nagpur**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Bengaluru**.
- **D: Pune** — Incorrect: it identifies **Pune**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Bengaluru**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**indian cities on longitude closest to delhi**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q30"></a>
## Q30. ILO Conventions 138 and 182 subject matter
**Primary route:** Social Justice → [Children and Child Protection](../../topics/social-justice/children-and-child-protection.md) → ILO Conventions 138 and 182 subject matter
**Primary classification:** Social Justice → Children and Child Protection → ILO Conventions 138 and 182 subject matter  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q30 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Social-Justice/basic/06_Children-and-Child-Protection](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Social-Justice/basic/06_Children-and-Child-Protection.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `social-justice-ilo-conventions-138-and-182-subject-matter`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

International Labour Organization's Conventions 138 and 182 are related to

(a) Child labour  
(b) Adaptation of agricultural practices to global climate change  
(c) Regulation of food prices and food security  
(d) Gender parity at the workplace  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Child labour**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The International Labour Organization (ILO) Conventions 138 and 182 are foundational instruments in the fight against child labour. Convention No. 138, known as the Minimum Age Convention, 1973, aims to achieve the effective abolition of child labour by requiring member states to establish a minimum age for admission to employment or work, not less than the age of completion of compulsory schooling and, in any case, not less than 15 years. Convention No. 182, the Worst Forms of Child Labour Convention, 1999, calls for the prohibition and elimination of the worst forms of child labour, including slavery, forced labour, debt bondage, serfdom, illicit activities, and hazardous work, as a matter of urgency. Both conventions are among the eight fundamental conventions of the ILO. India ratified both Conventions 138 and 182 in 2017, underscoring its commitment to eliminating child labour. The other options are incorrect: 'Adaptation of agricultural practices to global climate change' falls under environmental and climate change agreements, not specific ILO conventions. 'Regulation of food prices and food security' is addressed by international bodies like the FAO and national policies, not directly by these ILO conventions. 'Gender parity at the workplace' is a broad labour rights issue covered by other ILO conventions (e.g., Equal Remuneration Convention, Discrimination (Employment and Occupation) Convention) but not specifically 138 and 182.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Child labour** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: Adaptation of agricultural practices to global climate change** — Incorrect: it identifies **Adaptation of agricultural practices to global climate change**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Child labour**.
- **C: Regulation of food prices and food security** — Incorrect: it identifies **Regulation of food prices and food security**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Child labour**.
- **D: Gender parity at the workplace** — Incorrect: it identifies **Gender parity at the workplace**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Child labour**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**ilo conventions 138 and 182 subject matter**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.
