<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q31"></a>
## Q31. Money Bill constitutional provisions and definitions India
**Primary route:** Polity → [Parliament](../../topics/polity/parliament.md) → Money Bill constitutional provisions and definitions India
**Primary classification:** Polity → Parliament → Money Bill constitutional provisions and definitions India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q31 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Parliament](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Parliament.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-money-bill-constitutional-provisions-and-definitions-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Regarding Money Bill, which of the following statements is not correct?

(a) A bill shall be deemed to be a Money Bill if it contains only provisions relating to imposition, abolition, remission, alteration or regulation of any tax.  
(b) A Money Bill has provisions for the custody of the Consolidated Fund of India or the Contingency Fund of India.  
(c) A Money Bill is concerned with the appropriation of moneys out of the Contingency Fund of India.  
(d) A Money Bill deals with the regulation of borrowing of money or giving of any guarantee by the Government of India.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — A Money Bill is concerned with the appropriation of moneys out of the Contingency Fund of India.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question asks for the statement that is NOT correct regarding a Money Bill. Article 110(1) of the Indian Constitution defines a Money Bill. Let's analyze each option: This statement is correct. Article 110(1)(a) states that a bill is a Money Bill if it deals with "the imposition, abolition, remission, alteration or regulation of any tax." This statement is correct. Article 110(1)(c) includes provisions relating to "the custody of the Consolidated Fund or the Contingency Fund of India, the payment of moneys into or the withdrawal of moneys from any such Fund." This statement is correct. Article 110(1)(b) includes provisions relating to "the regulation of the borrowing of money or the giving of any guarantee by the Government of India, or the amendment of the law with respect to any financial obligations undertaken or to be undertaken by the Government of India."

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: A bill shall be deemed to be a Money Bill if it contains only provisions relating to imposition, abolition, remission, alteration or regulation of any tax.** — Incorrect: it identifies **A bill shall be deemed to be a Money Bill if it contains only provisions relating to imposition, abolition, remission, alteration or regulation of any tax.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **A Money Bill is concerned with the appropriation of moneys out of the Contingency Fund of India.**.
- **B: A Money Bill has provisions for the custody of the Consolidated Fund of India or the Contingency Fund of India.** — Incorrect: it identifies **A Money Bill has provisions for the custody of the Consolidated Fund of India or the Contingency Fund of India.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **A Money Bill is concerned with the appropriation of moneys out of the Contingency Fund of India.**.
- **C: A Money Bill is concerned with the appropriation of moneys out of the Contingency Fund of India.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: A Money Bill deals with the regulation of borrowing of money or giving of any guarantee by the Government of India.** — Incorrect: it identifies **A Money Bill deals with the regulation of borrowing of money or giving of any guarantee by the Government of India.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **A Money Bill is concerned with the appropriation of moneys out of the Contingency Fund of India.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**money bill constitutional provisions and definitions india**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

With reference to the election of the President of India, consider the following statements:
1. The value of the vote of each MLA varies from State to State.
2. The value of the vote of MPs of the Lok Sabha is more than the value of the vote of MPs of the Rajya Sabha.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The election of the President of India is conducted by an electoral college consisting of elected members of both Houses of Parliament and the elected members of the Legislative Assemblies of the States (and NCTs of Delhi and Puducherry). Statement 1: The value of the vote of each MLA (Member of Legislative Assembly) varies from State to State. This is correct. The value of an MLA's vote is calculated using a formula: (Total population of the State / (Total number of elected MLAs in the State Legislative Assembly * 1000)). Since the population and the number of elected MLAs differ for each state, the value of an MLA's vote naturally varies from one state to another.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**

- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q33"></a>
## Q33. India IAEA Additional Protocol ratification and implications
**Primary route:** International Relations → [Regional, Global and Minilateral Groupings](../../topics/international-relations/regional-global-and-minilateral-groupings.md) → India IAEA Additional Protocol ratification and implications
**Primary classification:** International Relations → Regional Global and Minilateral Groupings → India IAEA Additional Protocol ratification and implications  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q33 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [International-Relations/basic/10_Regional-Global-and-Minilateral-Groupings](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/International-Relations/basic/10_Regional-Global-and-Minilateral-Groupings.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `international-relations-india-iaea-additional-protocol-ratification-and-implications`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the Indian context, what is the implication of ratifying the 'Additional Protocol' with the 'International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA)'?

(a) The civilian nuclear reactors come under IAEA safeguards.  
(b) The military nuclear installations come under the inspection of IAEA.  
(c) The country will have the privilege to buy uranium from the Nuclear Suppliers Group (NSG).  
(d) The country automatically becomes a member of the NSG.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — The civilian nuclear reactors come under IAEA safeguards.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is A. India's ratification of the Additional Protocol with the International Atomic Energy Agency (IAEA) implies that its civilian nuclear reactors come under IAEA safeguards. India signed the Additional Protocol in 2009 and it entered into force in July 2014. This protocol grants the IAEA broader rights of access to information and locations, thereby providing greater assurance regarding the absence of undeclared nuclear material and activities in India's civilian nuclear program. This is part of India's commitment to its separation plan for civilian and military nuclear facilities, where only the civilian facilities are subject to IAEA safeguards. Option B is incorrect because India maintains a clear distinction between its civilian and military nuclear facilities. Its military nuclear installations remain outside the purview of IAEA inspection and safeguards, which is crucial for its strategic autonomy and nuclear deterrence. The Additional Protocol explicitly applies only to declared civilian nuclear activities.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: The civilian nuclear reactors come under IAEA safeguards.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: The military nuclear installations come under the inspection of IAEA.** — Incorrect: it identifies **The military nuclear installations come under the inspection of IAEA.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The civilian nuclear reactors come under IAEA safeguards.**.
- **C: The country will have the privilege to buy uranium from the Nuclear Suppliers Group (NSG).** — Incorrect: it identifies **The country will have the privilege to buy uranium from the Nuclear Suppliers Group (NSG).**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The civilian nuclear reactors come under IAEA safeguards.**.
- **D: The country automatically becomes a member of the NSG.** — Incorrect: it identifies **The country automatically becomes a member of the NSG.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The civilian nuclear reactors come under IAEA safeguards.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**india iaea additional protocol ratification and implications**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. GACSA is an outcome of the Climate Summit held in Paris in 2015.
2. Membership of GACSA does not create any binding obligations.
3. India was instrumental in the creation of GACSA.
Select the correct answer using the code given below: With reference to the 'Global Alliance for Climate-Smart Agriculture (GACSA)', which of the following statements is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The Global Alliance for Climate-Smart Agriculture (GACSA) was launched at the UN Climate Summit in 2014, not the Paris Climate Summit (COP21) in 2015. The Paris Agreement was adopted in December 2015, while GACSA's formation predates it, emphasizing a voluntary alliance launched earlier to address food security and climate change challenges. Statement 2 is correct. GACSA is described as an inclusive, voluntary, and action-oriented multi-stakeholder platform. Its framework clearly states that membership in the Alliance does not create any binding obligations, and each member individually determines the nature of their participation. This voluntary nature is a key characteristic of GACSA, allowing for broad participation from various stakeholders without imposing legal commitments.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q38"></a>
## Q38. Government of India Act 1935 residuary powers allocation
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Government of India Act 1935 and Congress Ministries](../../topics/modern-history/government-of-india-act-1935-and-congress-ministries.md) → Government of India Act 1935 residuary powers allocation
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Government of India Act 1935 and Congress Ministries → Government of India Act 1935 residuary powers allocation  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q38 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/24_Government-of-India-Act-1935-and-Congress-Ministries](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/24_Government-of-India-Act-1935-and-Congress-Ministries.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-government-of-india-act-1935-residuary-powers-allocation`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In the Federation established by The Government of India Act of 1935, residuary powers were given to the

(a) Federal Legislature  
(b) Governor General  
(c) Provincial Legislature  
(d) Provincial Governors  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Governor General**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Government of India Act of 1935 was a significant step towards a federal structure for British India. Under this Act, legislative powers were divided into three lists: Federal, Provincial, and Concurrent. However, for any subjects not included in these lists, i.e., residuary powers, the authority to legislate was explicitly vested in the Governor-General. This was a crucial feature highlighting the control retained by the British Crown through its representative. Options A and C are incorrect because neither the Federal Legislature nor the Provincial Legislatures were granted residuary powers; their legislative domain was restricted to the subjects enumerated in their respective lists. Option D, Provincial Governors, were executive heads of provinces and did not possess legislative powers, much less residuary powers. The Governor-General, as the head of the federal executive, held these discretionary powers.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Federal Legislature** — Incorrect: it identifies **Federal Legislature**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Governor General**.
- **B: Governor General** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: Provincial Legislature** — Incorrect: it identifies **Provincial Legislature**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Governor General**.
- **D: Provincial Governors** — Incorrect: it identifies **Provincial Governors**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Governor General**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**government of india act 1935 residuary powers allocation**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q39"></a>
## Q39. Speaker of Legislative Assembly office vacation provisions
**Primary route:** Polity → [State Legislatures](../../topics/polity/state-legislatures.md) → Speaker of Legislative Assembly office vacation provisions
**Primary classification:** Polity → State Legislature → Speaker of Legislative Assembly office vacation provisions  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q39 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/State-Legislature](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/State-Legislature.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-speaker-of-legislative-assembly-office-vacation-provisions`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Speaker of the Legislative Assembly shall vacate his/her office if he/she ceases to be a member of the Assembly.
2. Whenever the Legislative Assembly is dissolved, the Speaker shall vacate his/her office immediately.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. Article 179(a) of the Indian Constitution clearly states that a Speaker or a Deputy Speaker of a Legislative Assembly 'shall vacate his office if he ceases to be a member of the Assembly'. This is a fundamental prerequisite for holding the office; the Speaker must be a member of the House they preside over. Statement 2 is incorrect. The proviso to Article 179 of the Indian Constitution explicitly states: 'Provided that whenever the Assembly is dissolved, the Speaker shall not vacate his office until immediately before the first meeting of the Assembly after the dissolution.' This provision ensures continuity of the office of the Speaker even after the dissolution of the Assembly, until a new Assembly is constituted and its first meeting is held. This prevents a vacuum in the office and allows for the smooth conduct of the first session of the newly elected Assembly, including the administration of oath to new members and the election of the new Speaker. only statement 1 is correct.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**speaker of legislative assembly office vacation provisions**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following reflects the most appropriate relationship between law and liberty?

(a) If there are more laws, there is less liberty.  
(b) If there are no laws, there is no liberty.  
(c) If there is liberty, laws have to be made by the people.  
(d) If laws are changed too often, liberty is in danger.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — If there are no laws, there is no liberty.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is B. This option reflects the fundamental and most appropriate relationship between law and liberty in a structured society. Liberty, in a meaningful sense, is not merely the absence of restraints (negative liberty) but also the presence of conditions that enable individuals to exercise their rights and pursue their well-being. Without laws, society would descend into a 'state of nature' or anarchy, where the strong could dominate the weak, and there would be no protection for individual rights or freedoms. Laws provide the framework for order, justice, and the protection of rights, thus creating the necessary conditions for true liberty to exist and flourish.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: If there are more laws, there is less liberty.** — Incorrect: it identifies **If there are more laws, there is less liberty.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **If there are no laws, there is no liberty.**.
- **B: If there are no laws, there is no liberty.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: If there is liberty, laws have to be made by the people.** — Incorrect: it identifies **If there is liberty, laws have to be made by the people.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **If there are no laws, there is no liberty.**.
- **D: If laws are changed too often, liberty is in danger.** — Incorrect: it identifies **If laws are changed too often, liberty is in danger.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **If there are no laws, there is no liberty.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**conceptual relationship between law and liberty**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q41"></a>
## Q41. Governor of State criminal proceedings and emoluments protection
**Primary route:** Polity → [Governor, Chief Minister & State Council of Ministers](../../topics/polity/governor-and-cm.md) → Governor of State criminal proceedings and emoluments protection
**Primary classification:** Polity → Governor and CM → Governor of State criminal proceedings and emoluments protection  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q41 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. No criminal proceedings shall be instituted against the Governor of a State in any court during his term of office.
2. The emoluments and allowances of the Governor of a State shall not be diminished during his term of office.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. Article 361 (Protection of President and Governors and Rajpramukhs) of the Indian Constitution provides immunity to the President and the Governors from criminal proceedings. Clause (2) of Article 361 states: "No criminal proceedings whatsoever shall be instituted or continued against the President, or the Governor of a State, in any court during his term of office." This immunity ensures that these high constitutional functionaries can discharge their duties without fear of harassment through frivolous litigation.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: its combination does not match the verified truth-set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**governor of state criminal proceedings and emoluments protection**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q42"></a>
## Q42. Bani Thani painting and its Rajasthani school attribution
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Painting Traditions](../../topics/art-and-culture/painting-traditions.md) → Bani Thani painting and its Rajasthani school attribution
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Painting Traditions → Bani Thani painting and its Rajasthani school attribution  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q42 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/07_Painting-Traditions](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/07_Painting-Traditions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-bani-thani-painting-and-its-rajasthani-school-attribution`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The well-known painting "Bani Thani" belongs to the

(a) Bundi school  
(b) Jaipur school  
(c) Kangra school  
(d) Kishangarh school  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Kishangarh school**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The painting 'Bani Thani' is a widely recognized Indian miniature painting that originated from the Kishangarh school of painting. It was painted by Nihal Chand, an artist from the Marwar school of Kishangarh, during the reign of Sawant Singh (also known as Nagari Das) in the 18th century. Bani Thani is celebrated for its distinctive features, including elongated faces, almond-shaped eyes, arched eyebrows, and pointed chins, which are characteristic of the Kishangarh style. Let's look at why other options are incorrect: Bundi school (Option A): Known for its rich depiction of lush vegetation, dramatic night skies, and a unique way of depicting water. It often features hunting scenes, court life, and elements of nature. Jaipur school (Option B): Originated in Amer and later flourished in Jaipur. It was significantly influenced by the Mughal school, focusing on realistic portrayal of human figures, often featuring round faces and long hair for women. Kangra school (Option C): A prominent school of Pahari painting, known for its lyrical quality, delicate lines, brilliance of colors, and minuteness of decorative details. It often depicts Radha-Krishna themes, with soft and delicate colors. 'Bani Thani' with its unique stylistic elements is definitively associated with the Kishangarh school.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Bundi school** — Incorrect: it identifies **Bundi school**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Kishangarh school**.
- **B: Jaipur school** — Incorrect: it identifies **Jaipur school**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Kishangarh school**.
- **C: Kangra school** — Incorrect: it identifies **Kangra school**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Kishangarh school**.
- **D: Kishangarh school** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**bani thani painting and its rajasthani school attribution**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q43. THAAD Terminal High Altitude Area Defense system description
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Defence R&D, DRDO and Missile Systems](../../topics/science-and-technology/defence-randd-drdo-and-missile-systems.md) → THAAD Terminal High Altitude Area Defense system description
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Defence RandD DRDO and Missile Systems → THAAD Terminal High Altitude Area Defense system description  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q43 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/06_Defence-RandD-DRDO-and-Missile-Systems](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/06_Defence-RandD-DRDO-and-Missile-Systems.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-thaad-terminal-high-altitude-area-defense-system-description`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

What is "Terminal High Altitude Area Defense (THAAD)", sometimes seen in the news?

(a) An Israeli radar system  
(b) India's indigenous anti-missile programme  
(c) An American anti-missile system  
(d) A defence collaboration between Japan and South Korea  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — An American anti-missile system**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Terminal High Altitude Area Defense (THAAD) is a mobile anti-ballistic missile system developed and operated by the United States Army. It is designed to intercept and destroy short, medium, and intermediate-range ballistic missiles in their terminal phase (descent or re-entry) using a 'hit-to-kill' technology, meaning it destroys the incoming missile by direct collision, without an explosive warhead. The THAAD system was developed by Lockheed Martin. It has been a prominent topic in international news, particularly due to its deployment in South Korea in response to North Korea's missile threats, which has caused significant tension with China. Option A is incorrect. While Israel possesses advanced missile defense systems like Iron Dome and Arrow, THAAD is not an Israeli system. Option B is incorrect. India has its own indigenous Ballistic Missile Defence (BMD) Program, which includes systems like the Prithvi Air Defence (PAD) and Advanced Air Defence (AAD) interceptors, but THAAD is not part of India's program.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: An Israeli radar system** — Incorrect: it identifies **An Israeli radar system**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **An American anti-missile system**.
- **B: India's indigenous anti-missile programme** — Incorrect: it identifies **India's indigenous anti-missile programme**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **An American anti-missile system**.
- **C: An American anti-missile system** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: A defence collaboration between Japan and South Korea** — Incorrect: it identifies **A defence collaboration between Japan and South Korea**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **An American anti-missile system**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**thaad terminal high altitude area defense system description**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

## Q44. Tyagaraja Kritis Annamacharya kirtanas facts in classical music
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Indian Music](../../topics/art-and-culture/indian-music.md) → Tyagaraja Kritis Annamacharya kirtanas facts in classical music
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Indian Music → Tyagaraja Kritis Annamacharya kirtanas facts in classical music  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q44 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to cultural history of India, consider the following statements:
1. Most of the Tyagaraja Kritis are devotional songs in praise of Lord Krishna.
2. Tyagaraja created several new ragas.
3. Annamacharya and Tyagaraja are contemporaries.
4. Annamacharya kirtanas are devotional songs in praise of Lord Venkateshwara. Which of the statements given above are correct?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 and 4 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 3  
(d) 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 4 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1: Most of the Tyagaraja Kritis are devotional songs in praise of Lord Krishna. This statement is incorrect. Tyagaraja (1767-1847 AD) was one of the greatest composers of Carnatic music and a devout devotee of Lord Rama. His kritis (compositions) are overwhelmingly in praise of Lord Rama, with only a very small number dedicated to other deities. Statement 2: Tyagaraja created several new ragas. This statement is correct. Tyagaraja is celebrated not only for his prolific compositions but also for his innovation in music. He is credited with composing in many rare ragas and also creating several new ragas, enriching the Carnatic music tradition significantly. Statement 3: Annamacharya and Tyagaraja are contemporaries.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2, 4.
- **B: 2 and 4 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 4.
- **D: 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**tyagaraja kritis annamacharya kirtanas facts in classical music**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q45"></a>
## Q45. Rule of Law main features and principles
**Primary route:** Polity → [Salient Features and Constitutional Parts](../../topics/polity/salient-features-and-constitutional-parts.md) → Rule of Law main features and principles
**Primary classification:** Polity → Salient Features → Rule of Law main features and principles  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q45 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Salient-Features](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Salient-Features.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-rule-of-law-main-features-and-principles`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Limitation of powers
2. Equality before law
3. People's responsibility to the Government
4. Liberty and civil rights
Select the correct answer using the code given below: Which of the following are regarded as the main features of the "Rule of Law"?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 and 4 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 4 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1, 2 and 4 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The concept of 'Rule of Law' was propounded by A.V. Dicey, a British jurist. According to Dicey, the Rule of Law has three main implications or features: Supremacy of Law (Limitation of powers): This means that no man is punishable or can be lawfully made to suffer in body or goods except for a distinct breach of law established in the ordinary legal manner before the ordinary courts of the land. It signifies the absence of arbitrary power and that the government itself is subjected to the law. 'Limitation of powers' (Option 1) is a core feature. Equality before Law: This implies the equal subjection of all classes to the ordinary law of the land administered by the ordinary law courts. No one is above the law, regardless of their position or status. 'Equality before law' (Option 2) is a fundamental feature. Predominance of Legal Spirit (Liberty and civil rights): Dicey held that the general principles of the constitution (like liberty and civil rights) are the result of judicial decisions determining the rights of private persons in particular cases brought before the courts, rather than from a pre-defined written constitution. This ensures that individual liberties and civil rights are protected and enforced through the ordinary legal process. 'Liberty and civil rights' (Option 4) are indeed a feature derived from the spirit of the rule of law. People's responsibility to the Government: This statement (Option 3) is not considered a direct or main feature of the 'Rule of Law' as defined by Dicey. The Rule of Law primarily focuses on the government's subjection to law and the protection of citizens' rights from arbitrary state action. While citizens have civic responsibilities in a democratic society, the 'Rule of Law' itself is about the law governing the government and its people, not primarily about the people's responsibility to the government in the sense of a defining characteristic of the concept. It's more about the state's accountability to the law. options 1, 2, and 4 are correct, making option C the right answer.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 2, 4.
- **B: 2 and 4 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: 1, 2 and 4 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: 1, 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**rule of law main features and principles**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q46"></a>
## Q46. Legal tender money definition and characteristics
**Primary route:** Economy → [RBI, Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management](../../topics/economy/rbi-monetary-policy-and-liquidity-management.md) → Legal tender money definition and characteristics
**Primary classification:** Economy → RBI Monetary Policy and Liquidity Management → Legal tender money definition and characteristics  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q46 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: single-best answer · factual discrimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/04_RBI-Monetary-Policy-and-Liquidity-Management.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-legal-tender-money-definition-and-characteristics`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following statements correctly describes the meaning of legal tender money?

(a) The money which is tendered in courts of law to defray the fee of legal cases  
(b) The money which a creditor is under compulsion to accept in settlement of his claims  
(c) The bank money in the form of cheques, drafts, bills of exchange, etc.  
(d) The metallic money in circulation in a country  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — The money which a creditor is under compulsion to accept in settlement of his claims**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Legal tender money refers to any form of payment that is recognized by a legal system to be valid for meeting a financial obligation or debt. The key characteristic of legal tender is that a creditor cannot refuse to accept it as payment for a debt without legal consequences. In India, currency notes issued by the Reserve Bank of India (RBI) and coins issued by the Government of India are legal tender. option (B) accurately captures this essential feature: a creditor is legally compelled to accept it in settlement of claims. Option (A) is incorrect as legal tender is not exclusively for court fees; it's for all financial obligations. Court fees can typically be paid through legal tender, but the definition is much broader.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: The money which is tendered in courts of law to defray the fee of legal cases** — Incorrect: it identifies **The money which is tendered in courts of law to defray the fee of legal cases**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The money which a creditor is under compulsion to accept in settlement of his claims**.
- **B: The money which a creditor is under compulsion to accept in settlement of his claims** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: The bank money in the form of cheques, drafts, bills of exchange, etc.** — Incorrect: it identifies **The bank money in the form of cheques, drafts, bills of exchange, etc.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The money which a creditor is under compulsion to accept in settlement of his claims**.
- **D: The metallic money in circulation in a country** — Incorrect: it identifies **The metallic money in circulation in a country**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The money which a creditor is under compulsion to accept in settlement of his claims**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**legal tender money definition and characteristics**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q47"></a>
## Q47. Opportunity cost when government provides commodity free
**Primary route:** Economy → [Union Budget, Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators](../../topics/economy/union-budget-fiscal-policy-and-deficit-indicators.md) → Opportunity cost when government provides commodity free
**Primary classification:** Economy → Union Budget Fiscal Policy and Deficit Indicators → Opportunity cost when government provides commodity free  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q47 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/09_Union-Budget-Fiscal-Policy-and-Deficit-Indicators.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-opportunity-cost-when-government-provides-commodity-free`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

If a commodity is provided free to the public by the Government, then

(a) the opportunity cost is zero.  
(b) the opportunity cost is ignored.  
(c) the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the tax-paying public.  
(d) the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the Government.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the tax-paying public.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

In economics, nothing is truly 'free'. Every good or service produced requires the use of scarce resources (land, labor, capital, entrepreneurship), and these resources have alternative uses. The 'opportunity cost' is the value of the next best alternative that must be foregone to obtain something. When the government provides a commodity 'free' to the public, it means the direct consumers do not pay for it out of their pockets at the point of consumption. Option A is incorrect because resources are still used to produce the commodity, and these resources could have been used to produce something else. the opportunity cost is never zero. Option B is incorrect because while the direct consumers might not perceive the cost, the opportunity cost is still very much present and accounted for in the broader economic system. It's not ignored, rather it's borne by another entity.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the opportunity cost is zero.** — Incorrect: it identifies **the opportunity cost is zero.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the tax-paying public.**.
- **B: the opportunity cost is ignored.** — Incorrect: it identifies **the opportunity cost is ignored.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the tax-paying public.**.
- **C: the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the tax-paying public.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the Government.** — Incorrect: it identifies **the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the Government.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **the opportunity cost is transferred from the consumers of the product to the tax-paying public.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**opportunity cost when government provides commodity free**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q48"></a>
## Q48. GNP per capita not connoting economic development conditions
**Primary route:** Economy → [Growth, Development, HDI, IHDI and MPI](../../topics/economy/growth-development-hdi-ihdi-and-mpi.md) → GNP per capita not connoting economic development conditions
**Primary classification:** Economy → Growth Development HDI IHDI and MPI → GNP per capita not connoting economic development conditions  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q48 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/02_Growth-Development-HDI-IHDI-and-MPI](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/02_Growth-Development-HDI-IHDI-and-MPI.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-gnp-per-capita-not-connoting-economic-development-conditions`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Increase in absolute and per capita real GNP do not connote a higher level of economic development, if

(a) industrial output fails to keep pace with agricultural output.  
(b) agricultural output fails to keep pace with industrial output.  
(c) poverty and unemployment increase.  
(d) imports grow faster than exports.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — poverty and unemployment increase.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Economic growth, typically measured by an increase in Gross National Product (GNP) or Gross Domestic Product (GDP), refers to a quantitative increase in the output of goods and services. However, economic development is a much broader and qualitative concept that encompasses improvements in the overall well-being of the population, including better living standards, reduced poverty, greater employment opportunities, and more equitable distribution of income. If absolute and per capita real GNP increases, it indicates economic growth. But if, simultaneously, poverty and unemployment also increase (Option C), it implies that the benefits of this growth are not reaching a significant portion of the population. This scenario is often termed 'jobless growth' or 'ruthless growth,' where the rich get richer, and the poor remain poor or even become poorer. despite growth, there is no true economic development in terms of improved living standards for the masses.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: industrial output fails to keep pace with agricultural output.** — Incorrect: it identifies **industrial output fails to keep pace with agricultural output.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **poverty and unemployment increase.**.
- **B: agricultural output fails to keep pace with industrial output.** — Incorrect: it identifies **agricultural output fails to keep pace with industrial output.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **poverty and unemployment increase.**.
- **C: poverty and unemployment increase.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: imports grow faster than exports.** — Incorrect: it identifies **imports grow faster than exports.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **poverty and unemployment increase.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**gnp per capita not connoting economic development conditions**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q49"></a>
## Q49. Human capital formation concept and its meaning
**Primary route:** Economy → [Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend](../../topics/economy/employment-labour-codes-skills-and-demographic-dividend.md) → Human capital formation concept and its meaning
**Primary classification:** Economy → Employment Labour Codes Skills and Demographic Dividend → Human capital formation concept and its meaning  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q49 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-human-capital-formation-concept-and-its-meaning`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements: Human capital formation as a concept is better explained in terms of a process which enables
1. individuals of a country to accumulate more capital.
2. increasing the knowledge, skill levels and capacities of the people of the country.
3. accumulation of tangible wealth.
4. accumulation of intangible wealth.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 4  
(d) 1, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 2 and 4**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Human capital formation refers to the process of acquiring and increasing the number of persons with skills, education, and experience which are critical for the economic and political development of a country. It is an investment in human beings and involves expenditures on education, health, and on-the-job training. Let's evaluate each statement: individuals of a country to accumulate more capital: While human capital formation can lead to higher productivity and income, which in turn enables individuals to accumulate more physical or financial capital, this is a consequence, not the core process of human capital formation itself. The concept primarily focuses on enhancing human attributes, not directly accumulating financial or physical assets for individuals. increasing the knowledge, skill levels and capacities of the people of the country: This statement accurately defines the essence of human capital formation. Investments in education, health, and training directly lead to an increase in knowledge, skills, and overall capacities of the workforce, making them more productive. accumulation of tangible wealth: Tangible wealth refers to physical assets like land, buildings, machinery, and infrastructure. Human capital, comprising knowledge, skills, and health, is inherently an intangible asset that resides within individuals. this statement is incorrect. accumulation of intangible wealth: Human capital is a form of intangible wealth. Knowledge, skills, health, and acquired expertise are non-physical assets that enhance an individual's productive capacity and societal well-being. This statement correctly describes an outcome of the human capital formation process. Based on this analysis, statements 2 and 4 are correct.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1; omits supported statement(s) 4.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 4.
- **C: 2 and 4** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: 1, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**human capital formation concept and its meaning**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q50"></a>
## Q50. High saving economy low output and capital-output ratio
**Primary route:** Economy → [National Income, GDP, GVA and Measurement](../../topics/economy/national-income-gdp-gva-and-measurement.md) → High saving economy low output and capital-output ratio
**Primary classification:** Economy → National Income GDP GVA and Measurement → High saving economy low output and capital-output ratio  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q50 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/01_National-Income-GDP-GVA-and-Measurement](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/01_National-Income-GDP-GVA-and-Measurement.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-high-saving-economy-low-output-and-capital-output-ratio`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Despite being a high saving economy, capital formation may not result in significant increase in output due to

(a) weak administrative machinery  
(b) illiteracy  
(c) high population density  
(d) high capital-output ratio  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — high capital-output ratio**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is (D) high capital-output ratio. Capital formation refers to the net addition to the existing stock of capital assets (like machinery, buildings, infrastructure) in an economy, which is primarily funded by savings. A high saving economy implies that there is sufficient financial resource available for investment and capital formation. However, for this capital formation to translate into a significant increase in output (economic growth), the efficiency of capital utilization is crucial. The capital-output ratio measures the amount of capital required to produce one unit of output. A high capital-output ratio indicates that more capital is needed to generate a given amount of output, implying inefficiency in the use of capital. This inefficiency can arise from various factors such as outdated technology, poor infrastructure, delays in project implementation, underutilization of capacity, or a long gestation period for projects. even if an economy saves a lot and invests it, if its capital-output ratio is high, the resulting increase in production will be modest.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: weak administrative machinery** — Incorrect: it identifies **weak administrative machinery**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **high capital-output ratio**.
- **B: illiteracy** — Incorrect: it identifies **illiteracy**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **high capital-output ratio**.
- **C: high population density** — Incorrect: it identifies **high population density**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **high capital-output ratio**.
- **D: high capital-output ratio** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**high saving economy low output and capital-output ratio**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q51"></a>
## Q51. Colonial government measures after Santhal Uprising subsided
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Economic Impact of British Rule](../../topics/modern-history/economic-impact-of-british-rule.md) → Colonial government measures after Santhal Uprising subsided
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Economic Impact of British Rule → Colonial government measures after Santhal Uprising subsided  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q51 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-colonial-government-measures-after-santhal-uprising-subsided`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. The territories called 'Santhal Paraganas' were created.
2. It became illegal for a Santhal to transfer land to a non-Santhal.
Select the correct answer using the code given below: After the Santhal Uprising subsided, what was/were the measure/measures taken by the colonial government?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Both 1 and 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Santhal Uprising (1855-1856), led by Sidhu and Kanhu Murmu, was a significant tribal rebellion against the oppressive zamindari system, moneylenders, and colonial exploitation. After the rebellion was brutally suppressed by the British, the colonial government, aiming to pacify the region and prevent future uprisings, took several administrative and legislative measures. Statement 1: 'The territories called 'Santhal Paraganas' were created.' This statement is correct. Following the uprising, the British government passed Act XXXVII of 1855, which created a separate administrative unit known as 'Santhal Parganas'. This was a non-regulation district with special laws to protect the Santhals from exploitation and to maintain peace. This measure aimed at giving the Santhals a sense of security and autonomy within a defined territory.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: Both 1 and 2** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: Neither 1 nor 2** — Incorrect: its combination does not match the verified truth-set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**colonial government measures after santhal uprising subsided**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q52"></a>
## Q52. Economic results of British rule in 19th century India
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Economic Impact of British Rule](../../topics/modern-history/economic-impact-of-british-rule.md) → Economic results of British rule in 19th century India
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Economic Impact of British Rule → Economic results of British rule in 19th century India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q52 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-economic-results-of-british-rule-in-19th-century-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Economically, one of the results of the British rule in India in the 19th century was the

(a) increase in the export of Indian handicrafts  
(b) growth in the number of Indian owned factories  
(c) commercialization of Indian agriculture  
(d) rapid increase in the urban population  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — commercialization of Indian agriculture**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is (C) commercialization of Indian agriculture. British rule in the 19th century fundamentally reoriented the Indian economy to serve the interests of the British Empire. This involved significant changes in the agricultural sector. Option (C) Commercialization of Indian agriculture: This was a defining feature of British economic policy. The British encouraged and often coerced Indian peasants to grow cash crops like indigo, cotton, jute, opium, and sugarcane instead of food grains. This was driven by the demand for raw materials for British industries and the need for agricultural exports to generate revenue and facilitate trade. Peasants were often forced into this through various means, including advances, land revenue demands, and market forces, leading to a shift from subsistence farming to production for the market. This made the Indian peasant vulnerable to market fluctuations and often led to food scarcity and famines. Option (A) increase in the export of Indian handicrafts: This is incorrect. The 19th century witnessed the decline and destruction of Indian handicrafts. British machine-made goods, imported cheaply due to 'one-way free trade' policies, outcompeted traditional Indian products. Furthermore, the British imposed discriminatory tariffs on Indian finished goods while allowing raw materials to be exported to Britain duty-free, leading to the de-industrialization of India. Option (B) growth in the number of Indian owned factories: This is largely incorrect for the 19th century as a general economic result. While some modern industries, like textiles, emerged towards the latter part of the 19th century, their growth was limited and faced significant competition and discriminatory policies from the British. The primary characteristic of the 19th-century economy under British rule was de-industrialization rather than robust industrial growth, especially in Indian-owned factories. Option (D) rapid increase in the urban population: This is also incorrect as a general economic outcome. While certain port cities and administrative centres like Calcutta, Bombay, and Madras grew, many traditional urban centres and towns that thrived on handicrafts and local trade declined due to de-industrialization.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: increase in the export of Indian handicrafts** — Incorrect: it identifies **increase in the export of Indian handicrafts**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **commercialization of Indian agriculture**.
- **B: growth in the number of Indian owned factories** — Incorrect: it identifies **growth in the number of Indian owned factories**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **commercialization of Indian agriculture**.
- **C: commercialization of Indian agriculture** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: rapid increase in the urban population** — Incorrect: it identifies **rapid increase in the urban population**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **commercialization of Indian agriculture**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**economic results of british rule in 19th century india**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q53"></a>
## Q53. Article 356 President's Rule consequences in a state
**Primary route:** Polity → [Emergency Provisions](../../topics/polity/emergency-provisions.md) → Article 356 President's Rule consequences in a state
**Primary classification:** Polity → Emergency Provisions → Article 356 President's Rule consequences in a state  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q53 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Polity/basic/Emergency-Provisions](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Polity/basic/Emergency-Provisions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `polity-article-356-president-s-rule-consequences-in-a-state`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

If the President of India exercises his power as provided under Article 356 of the Constitution in respect of a particular State, then

(a) the Assembly of the State is automatically dissolved.  
(b) the powers of the Legislature of that State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the Parliament.  
(c) Article 19 is suspended in that State.  
(d) the President can make laws relating to that State.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — the powers of the Legislature of that State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the Parliament.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Article 356 of the Indian Constitution deals with the imposition of 'President's Rule' in a state when there is a failure of constitutional machinery. Option A: 'the Assembly of the State is automatically dissolved.' This is incorrect. While the State Legislative Assembly can be dissolved under President's Rule, it is not an automatic consequence. It can also be merely suspended or placed in suspended animation, giving time for the situation to be reviewed or for a new government to be formed. The dissolution or suspension depends on the specific Proclamation by the President. Option B: 'the powers of the Legislature of that State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the Parliament.'

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: the Assembly of the State is automatically dissolved.** — Incorrect: it identifies **the Assembly of the State is automatically dissolved.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **the powers of the Legislature of that State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the Parliament.**.
- **B: the powers of the Legislature of that State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the Parliament.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: Article 19 is suspended in that State.** — Incorrect: it identifies **Article 19 is suspended in that State.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **the powers of the Legislature of that State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the Parliament.**.
- **D: the President can make laws relating to that State.** — Incorrect: it identifies **the President can make laws relating to that State.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **the powers of the Legislature of that State shall be exercisable by or under the authority of the Parliament.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**article 356 president's rule consequences in a state**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q54"></a>
## Q54. Regional textile craft heritage Puthukuli Sujni Uppada pairs
**Primary route:** Art and Culture → [Crafts, Textiles, Folk and Tribal Traditions](../../topics/art-and-culture/crafts-textiles-folk-and-tribal-traditions.md) → Regional textile craft heritage Puthukuli Sujni Uppada pairs
**Primary classification:** Art and Culture → Crafts Textiles Folk and Tribal Traditions → Regional textile craft heritage Puthukuli Sujni Uppada pairs  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q54 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: pair matching · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Indian-Art-and-Culture/basic/12_Crafts-Textiles-Folk-and-Tribal-Traditions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `art-and-culture-regional-textile-craft-heritage-puthukuli-sujni-uppada-pairs`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs: (Craft — Heritage of)
1. Puthukkuli shawls — Tamil Nadu
2. Sujni embroidery — Maharashtra
3. Uppada Jamdani saris — Karnataka Which of the pairs given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Puthukkuli shawls - Tamil Nadu : This pair is correct. Puthukkuli shawls, also known as Toda embroidery, are a traditional craft of the Toda tribe residing in the Nilgiri Hills of Tamil Nadu. The unique embroidery is done on unbleached cotton cloth using red and black woollen threads, primarily for shawls. Sujni embroidery - Maharashtra : This pair is incorrect. Sujni embroidery is a traditional folk art and quilt-making craft that originated in Bihar, particularly in the Bhusura village. It is known for its intricate stitches and narrative themes, often depicting daily life or mythological stories. This craft has also received a Geographical Indication (GI) tag. Uppada Jamdani saris - Karnataka : This pair is incorrect. Uppada Jamdani saris are a distinctive silk saree style woven in Uppada, a village in the East Godavari district of Andhra Pradesh. These sarees are known for their lightweight texture and intricate Jamdani weaving technique, inspired by the traditional Jamdani of Bangladesh. Uppada Jamdani saris also hold a GI tag.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **D: 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2, 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**regional textile craft heritage puthukuli sujni uppada pairs**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Mobile phone operations
2. Banking operations
3. Controlling the power grids
Select the correct answer using the code given below: In which of the following areas can GPS technology be used?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

GPS (Global Positioning System) technology is a satellite-based navigation system that provides accurate location, velocity, and time information. Its applications extend far beyond just navigation. Mobile phone operations: This is a primary and widely recognized application of GPS. Mobile phones use GPS for various location-based services such as mapping, navigation (e.g., Google Maps, ride-sharing apps), geotagging photos, finding nearby points of interest, and even for emergency services to locate a caller. Banking operations: While GPS is not directly involved in the transaction process itself, its precise timing component is crucial for banking and financial operations. Financial institutions rely on highly accurate time synchronization for timestamping transactions, ensuring data integrity, managing high-frequency trading, and maintaining cybersecurity across distributed networks. GPS-derived time signals are used by Network Time Protocol (NTP) and Precision Time Protocol (PTP) servers to synchronize clocks across their systems. Controlling the power grids: GPS plays a vital role in modern smart grids, particularly for time synchronization. Phasor Measurement Units (PMUs), which are critical for monitoring the health and stability of the power grid, require highly precise, synchronized time stamps to accurately measure electrical waves across a wide geographical area. This synchronization, often provided by GPS, enables real-time observation, fault detection, and precise control of the power system, helping to prevent blackouts and optimize grid performance. Given that all three statements describe legitimate and significant applications of GPS technology, the correct answer is 1, 2 and 3.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2, 3.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**gps technology applications in mobile banking power grids**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q56"></a>
## Q56. RBI management of government securities and treasury bills
**Primary route:** Economy → [Money Market, Capital Market and Financial Instruments](../../topics/economy/money-market-capital-market-and-financial-instruments.md) → RBI management of government securities and treasury bills
**Primary classification:** Economy → Money Market Capital Market and Financial Instruments → RBI management of government securities and treasury bills  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q56 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/07_Money-Market-Capital-Market-and-Financial-Instruments](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/07_Money-Market-Capital-Market-and-Financial-Instruments.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-rbi-management-of-government-securities-and-treasury-bills`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Reserve Bank of India manages and services Government of India Securities but not any State Government Securities.
2. Treasury bills are issued by the Government of India and there are no treasury bills issued by the State Governments.
3. Treasury bills offer are issued at a discount from the par value.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Reserve Bank of India manages and services Government of India Securities but not any State Government Securities. This statement is incorrect. The Reserve Bank of India (RBI) acts as a debt manager for both the Central Government and the State Governments. Under Sections 20 and 21 of the RBI Act, 1934, RBI is vested with the powers of managing public debt for the Government of India. Additionally, the RBI has entered into agreements with State Governments and Union Territories to manage their public debt, including the issuance, interest payments, and repayment of State Government securities (State Development Loans - SDLs). Treasury bills are issued by the Government of India and there are no treasury bills issued by the State Governments.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1; omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.
