<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q57"></a>
## Q57. Earth magnetic field reversal early atmosphere and life modification
**Primary route:** Geography → [The Earths Crust Rocks](../../topics/geography/the-earths-crust-rocks.md) → Earth magnetic field reversal early atmosphere and life modification
**Primary classification:** Geography → The Earths Crust Rocks → Earth magnetic field reversal early atmosphere and life modification  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q57 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Earth's magnetic field has reversed every few hundred thousand years.
2. When the Earth was created more than 4000 million years ago, there was 54% oxygen and no carbon dioxide.
3. When living organisms originated, they modified the early atmosphere of the Earth.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. Geological evidence from studies of paleomagnetism in rocks shows that the Earth's magnetic field has indeed reversed its polarity multiple times throughout geological history. The reversals are not regular but occur intermittently, with an average frequency of a few hundred thousand years, although the time between reversals can vary significantly, ranging from tens of thousands to millions of years. For example, the last reversal, the Brunhes-Matuyama reversal, occurred about 780,000 years ago.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q58"></a>
## Q58. WannaCry Petya EternalBlue terms in technology news
**Primary route:** Internal Security → [Cyber Security, Critical Information Infrastructure and Cybercrime](../../topics/internal-security/cyber-security-cii-and-cybercrime.md) → WannaCry Petya EternalBlue terms in technology news
**Primary classification:** Internal Security → Cyber Security CII and Cybercrime → WannaCry Petya EternalBlue terms in technology news  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q58 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Internal-Security/basic/08_Cyber-Security-CII-and-Cybercrime](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Internal-Security/basic/08_Cyber-Security-CII-and-Cybercrime.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `internal-security-wannacry-petya-eternalblue-terms-in-technology-news`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The terms 'WannaCry, Petya and EternalBlue' sometimes mentioned in the news recently are related to

(a) Exoplanets  
(b) Cryptocurrency  
(c) Cyber attacks  
(d) Mini satellites  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Cyber attacks**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The terms 'WannaCry,' 'Petya' (also known as NotPetya), and 'EternalBlue' are all directly related to major global cyber attacks that occurred in 2017. WannaCry was a widespread ransomware cryptoworm attack that targeted computers running Microsoft Windows in May 2017, encrypting user data and demanding ransom payments in Bitcoin. Petya (NotPetya) was another powerful cyberattack that began in June 2017, primarily affecting organizations in Ukraine but also spreading globally. While initially appearing as ransomware, it was later identified as a wiper, designed to destroy data rather than merely encrypt it for ransom. EternalBlue is a computer exploit developed by the U.S. National Security Agency (NSA) that was leaked by a group called 'Shadow Brokers' in April 2017. This exploit leveraged a vulnerability in Microsoft Windows' Server Message Block (SMB) protocol and was famously used by both WannaCry and NotPetya to propagate rapidly across networks. these terms are fundamentally linked to the field of cyber security and malicious digital activities. Options A (Exoplanets), B (Cryptocurrency), and D (Mini satellites) are entirely unrelated to the technical nature and context of these terms. While cyberattacks can sometimes involve cryptocurrency (as WannaCry demanded Bitcoin), cryptocurrency itself is not what these terms represent.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Exoplanets** — Incorrect: it identifies **Exoplanets**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Cyber attacks**.
- **B: Cryptocurrency** — Incorrect: it identifies **Cryptocurrency**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Cyber attacks**.
- **C: Cyber attacks** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: Mini satellites** — Incorrect: it identifies **Mini satellites**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Cyber attacks**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**wannacry petya eternalblue terms in technology news**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q59"></a>
## Q59. Conservation Agriculture practices minimum tillage and crop rotations
**Primary route:** Economy → [Irrigation, Inputs, Credit, Insurance and Sustainable Agriculture](../../topics/economy/irrigation-inputs-credit-insurance-and-sustainable-agriculture.md) → Conservation Agriculture practices minimum tillage and crop rotations
**Primary classification:** Economy → Irrigation Inputs Credit Insurance and Sustainable Agriculture → Conservation Agriculture practices minimum tillage and crop rotations  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q59 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/14_Irrigation-Inputs-Credit-Insurance-and-Sustainable-Agriculture.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-conservation-agriculture-practices-minimum-tillage-and-crop-rotations`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Avoiding the monoculture practices
2. Adopting minimum tillage
3. Avoiding the cultivation of plantation crops
4. Using crop residues to cover soil surface
5. Adopting spatial and temporal crop sequencing/crop rotations
Select the correct answer using the code given below: With reference to the circumstances in Indian agriculture, the concept of "Conservation Agriculture" assumes significance. Which of the following fall under the Conservation Agriculture?

(a) 1, 3 and 4  
(b) 2, 3, 4 and 5  
(c) 2, 4 and 5  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 5  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 2, 4 and 5**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Conservation Agriculture (CA) is a sustainable agricultural system based on three core principles, as defined by the Food and Agriculture Organization (FAO): Minimum mechanical soil disturbance (No-till or minimum tillage): This directly aligns with statement 2, "Adopting minimum tillage." Reducing tillage helps in maintaining soil structure, organic matter, and beneficial soil organisms. Permanent organic soil cover: This refers to keeping the soil surface covered with crop residues or living mulch. Statement 4, "Using crop residues to cover soil surface," directly reflects this principle. This practice helps in reducing erosion, conserving soil moisture, and suppressing weeds. Diversification of crop species grown in sequences and/or associations: This involves crop rotation and intercropping to break pest and disease cycles, improve nutrient cycling, and enhance biodiversity. Statement 5, "Adopting spatial and temporal crop sequencing/crop rotations," perfectly describes this principle. Now, let's evaluate the given statements: Statement 1: "Avoiding the monoculture practices" - While avoiding monoculture is an outcome and a key characteristic of sustainable agriculture and indirectly related to crop diversification (principle 3), statement 5 (crop sequencing/rotations) is a more direct and comprehensive description of the practice that achieves this goal within the core principles of CA. Statement 3: "Avoiding the cultivation of plantation crops" - This statement is incorrect. Conservation Agriculture principles relate to the methodology of farming (how crops are grown), not the type of crops cultivated. Plantation crops, like any other crop, can be grown using CA principles if suitable practices are applied. statements 2, 4, and 5 accurately represent the fundamental principles of Conservation Agriculture. Option C includes these three core principles, making it the correct answer.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **5: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2, 5.
- **B: 2, 3, 4 and 5** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.
- **C: 2, 4 and 5** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: 1, 2, 3 and 5** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 4.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**conservation agriculture practices minimum tillage and crop rotations**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q60"></a>
## Q60. Sixth mass extinction causes and human activities impact
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Biodiversity Levels and Hotspots](../../topics/environment/biodiversity-levels-and-hotspots.md) → Sixth mass extinction causes and human activities impact
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Biodiversity Levels and Hotspots → Sixth mass extinction causes and human activities impact  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q60 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/04_Biodiversity-Levels-and-Hotspots](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/04_Biodiversity-Levels-and-Hotspots.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-sixth-mass-extinction-causes-and-human-activities-impact`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The term "sixth mass extinction/sixth extinction" is often mentioned in the news in the context of the discussion of

(a) Widespread monoculture practices in agriculture and large-scale commercial farming with indiscriminate use of chemicals in many parts of the world that may result in the loss of good native ecosystems.  
(b) Fears of a possible collision of a meteorite with the Earth in the near future in the manner it happened 65 million years ago that caused the mass extinction of many species including those of dinosaurs.  
(c) Large scale cultivation of genetically modified crops in many parts of the world and promoting their cultivation in other parts of the world which may cause the disappearance of good native crop plants and the loss of food biodiversity.  
(d) Mankind's over-exploitation/misuse of natural resources, fragmentation/loss of natural habitats, destruction of ecosystems, pollution and global climate change.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Mankind's over-exploitation/misuse of natural resources, fragmentation/loss of natural habitats, destruction of ecosystems, pollution and global climate change.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The term "sixth mass extinction," also known as the Holocene extinction or Anthropocene extinction, refers to the ongoing extinction event of species that began roughly at the start of the Industrial Revolution due to human activities. This period is characterized by an exceptionally high rate of species extinction and is attributed primarily to anthropogenic factors. Option (D) accurately encapsulates the main drivers identified by scientists for this ongoing extinction. Mankind's over-exploitation and misuse of natural resources (like overfishing, deforestation), fragmentation and loss of natural habitats (due to urbanization, agriculture, infrastructure), destruction of ecosystems, widespread pollution (air, water, soil), and global climate change are the five major causes (often referred to as 'HIPPO' - Habitat destruction, Invasive species, Pollution, Population, Overexploitation, with climate change being a significant exacerbating factor) of biodiversity loss and the current extinction crisis.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Widespread monoculture practices in agriculture and large-scale commercial farming with indiscriminate use of chemicals in many parts of the world that may result in the loss of good native ecosystems.** — Incorrect: it identifies **Widespread monoculture practices in agriculture and large-scale commercial farming with indiscriminate use of chemicals in many parts of the world that may result in the loss of good native ecosystems.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Mankind's over-exploitation/misuse of natural resources, fragmentation/loss of natural habitats, destruction of ecosystems, pollution and global climate change.**.
- **B: Fears of a possible collision of a meteorite with the Earth in the near future in the manner it happened 65 million years ago that caused the mass extinction of many species including those of dinosaurs.** — Incorrect: it identifies **Fears of a possible collision of a meteorite with the Earth in the near future in the manner it happened 65 million years ago that caused the mass extinction of many species including those of dinosaurs.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Mankind's over-exploitation/misuse of natural resources, fragmentation/loss of natural habitats, destruction of ecosystems, pollution and global climate change.**.
- **C: Large scale cultivation of genetically modified crops in many parts of the world and promoting their cultivation in other parts of the world which may cause the disappearance of good native crop plants and the loss of food biodiversity.** — Incorrect: it identifies **Large scale cultivation of genetically modified crops in many parts of the world and promoting their cultivation in other parts of the world which may cause the disappearance of good native crop plants and the loss of food biodiversity.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Mankind's over-exploitation/misuse of natural resources, fragmentation/loss of natural habitats, destruction of ecosystems, pollution and global climate change.**.
- **D: Mankind's over-exploitation/misuse of natural resources, fragmentation/loss of natural habitats, destruction of ecosystems, pollution and global climate change.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**sixth mass extinction causes and human activities impact**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q61"></a>
## Q61. IRNSS satellite system orbits and coverage area India
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Satellites, NavIC, GAGAN and Applications](../../topics/science-and-technology/satellites-navic-gagan-and-applications.md) → IRNSS satellite system orbits and coverage area India
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Satellites NavIC GAGAN and Applications → IRNSS satellite system orbits and coverage area India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q61 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/02_Satellites-NavIC-GAGAN-and-Applications](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/02_Satellites-NavIC-GAGAN-and-Applications.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-irnss-satellite-system-orbits-and-coverage-area-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the Indian Regional Navigation Satellite System (IRNSS), consider the following statements:
1. IRNSS has three satellites in geostationary and four satellites in geosynchronous orbits.
2. IRNSS covers entire India and about 5500 sq. km beyond its borders.
3. India will have its own satellite navigation system with full global coverage by the middle of 2019.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 1 and 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) None  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1: IRNSS has three satellites in geostationary and four satellites in geosynchronous orbits. This statement is correct. The IRNSS constellation consists of seven satellites (with additional standby satellites). Of these, three satellites are positioned in Geostationary Earth Orbit (GEO) at specific longitudes, and four satellites are in Geosynchronous Earth Orbit (GSO) with an inclination of 29 degrees to the equatorial plane. This configuration ensures continuous visibility over the Indian subcontinent and its surrounding regions. Statement 2: IRNSS covers entire India and about 5500 sq. km beyond its borders. This statement is incorrect. NavIC is designed to provide accurate position information service to users in India and a region extending up to 1500 km (not 5500 sq. km) from its boundary. The figure '5500 sq. km' is a misrepresentation of the coverage area, which is defined by a radial distance of 1500 km from the Indian border.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2, 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **D: None** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**irnss satellite system orbits and coverage area india**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q62. Einstein General Theory of Relativity key scientific predictions
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Physics Fundamentals](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-physics-fundamentals.md) → Einstein General Theory of Relativity key scientific predictions
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science Physics Fundamentals → Einstein General Theory of Relativity key scientific predictions

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following phenomena :
1. Light is affected by gravity.
2. The Universe is constantly expanding.
3. Matter warps its surrounding space-time. Which of the above is/are the prediction/predictions of Albert Einstein's General Theory of Relativity, often discussed in media?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Albert Einstein's General Theory of Relativity (GR), published in 1915, revolutionized our understanding of gravity, proposing it not as a force but as a manifestation of the curvature of spacetime caused by mass and energy. Light is affected by gravity: This is a direct and famous prediction of General Relativity. According to GR, light, while massless, follows the shortest path (geodesic) through spacetime. If spacetime is curved by massive objects, light will appear to bend around them. This phenomenon, known as gravitational lensing, was experimentally confirmed by Arthur Eddington during a solar eclipse in 1919, observing the bending of starlight around the Sun. The Universe is constantly expanding: The General Theory of Relativity, when applied to the universe on large scales (cosmology), leads to solutions (like the Friedmann equations) that describe a dynamic universe, one that can be expanding, contracting, or static. Although Einstein initially introduced a 'cosmological constant' to force a static universe, he later retracted it after Edwin Hubble's observations in the late 1920s confirmed the universe is indeed expanding. The expansion of the universe is thus a natural consequence allowed by and described within the framework of GR. Matter warps its surrounding space-time: This is the foundational concept of General Relativity. Instead of gravity being a force pulling objects, GR posits that massive objects (and energy) curve or 'warp' the fabric of spacetime around them. Other objects, including light, then move along the 'straightest possible' paths (geodesics) in this curved spacetime, which we perceive as gravity. Since all three statements are either direct predictions or logical consequences/descriptions within Einstein's General Theory of Relativity, option D is the correct answer.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **B: 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1, 2.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**einstein general theory of relativity key scientific predictions**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q63"></a>
## Q63. GM mustard developed in India genes and properties
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Genetic Engineering, GM Crops and CRISPR](../../topics/science-and-technology/genetic-engineering-gm-crops-and-crispr.md) → GM mustard developed in India genes and properties
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Genetic Engineering GM Crops and CRISPR → GM mustard developed in India genes and properties  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q63 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/14_Genetic-Engineering-GM-Crops-and-CRISPR](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/14_Genetic-Engineering-GM-Crops-and-CRISPR.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-gm-mustard-developed-in-india-genes-and-properties`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the Genetically Modified mustard (GM mustard) developed in India, consider the following statements:
1. GM mustard has the genes of a soil bacterium that give the plant the property of pest-resistance to a wide variety of pests.
2. GM mustard has the genes that allow the plant cross-pollination and hybridization.
3. GM mustard has been developed jointly by the IARI and Punjab Agricultural University.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. Dhara Mustard Hybrid-11 (DMH-11), the GM mustard developed in India, is primarily a herbicide-tolerant crop, not a pest-resistant one. The genes incorporated (Barnase, Barstar, and Bar) facilitate hybrid seed production and confer tolerance to the herbicide glufosinate-ammonium. While 'Bt' crops (like Bt cotton) have genes from the soil bacterium for pest resistance, this is not the case for GM mustard (DMH-11). Statement 2 is correct. DMH-11 utilizes the Barnase-Barstar system, which is a genetic engineering approach designed to achieve controlled male sterility and subsequent fertility restoration in mustard. This system facilitates cross-pollination to produce hybrid seeds, which are known for higher yields due to hybrid vigor.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **B: 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**gm mustard developed in india genes and properties**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following pairs: (Terms sometimes seen in news — Context/Topic)
1. Belle II experiment — Artificial Intelligence
2. Blockchain technology — Digital/ Cryptocurrency
3. CRISPR-Cas9 — Particle Physics Which of the pairs given above is/are correctly matched ?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Belle II experiment | Artificial Intelligence: This pair is incorrectly matched. The Belle II experiment is a major international particle physics experiment conducted at the SuperKEKB accelerator in Japan. Its primary goal is to search for new physics beyond the Standard Model by studying B mesons (particles containing a bottom quark) and their decays. While advanced computational techniques, including machine learning or AI, might be used in data analysis for such experiments, Artificial Intelligence is not the 'context/topic' of the experiment itself. Its core field is particle physics. Blockchain technology | Digital/Cryptocurrency: This pair is correctly matched. Blockchain is a decentralized, distributed, and immutable ledger technology that underpins digital currencies like Bitcoin and Ethereum. It provides the secure and transparent framework for recording transactions without the need for a central authority. its primary context is indeed digital/cryptocurrency and broader digital transaction systems.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q65"></a>
## Q65. Carbon fertilization effect on plant growth and atmosphere
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Climate Change Science Greenhouse Effect](../../topics/environment/climate-change-science-greenhouse-effect.md) → Carbon fertilization effect on plant growth and atmosphere
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Climate Change Science Greenhouse Effect → Carbon fertilization effect on plant growth and atmosphere  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q65 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/17_Climate-Change-Science-Greenhouse-Effect](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/17_Climate-Change-Science-Greenhouse-Effect.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-carbon-fertilization-effect-on-plant-growth-and-atmosphere`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which of the following statements best describes "carbon fertilization"?

(a) Increased plant growth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere  
(b) Increased temperature of Earth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere  
(c) Increased acidity of oceans as a result of increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere  
(d) Adaptation of all living beings on Earth to the climate change brought about by the increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Increased plant growth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Carbon fertilization refers to the increased rate of photosynthesis in plants due to elevated levels of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere. Carbon dioxide (CO2) is a crucial input for photosynthesis, the process by which plants convert light energy into chemical energy, producing sugars for growth. As atmospheric CO2 concentrations have risen due to human activities, many plants, particularly C3 plants (which include most trees and agricultural crops like rice, wheat, and soybeans), show an enhanced growth rate because CO2 availability is no longer a limiting factor for their photosynthetic process. This phenomenon can lead to increased biomass production and is sometimes seen as a 'greening' effect on the planet, although its long-term implications for ecosystems are complex and not uniformly beneficial. Option B describes the greenhouse effect and global warming, where increased CO2 traps more heat, leading to a rise in Earth's temperature. This is a consequence of increased CO2, but not 'carbon fertilization'.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Increased plant growth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: Increased temperature of Earth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere** — Incorrect: it identifies **Increased temperature of Earth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Increased plant growth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere**.
- **C: Increased acidity of oceans as a result of increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere** — Incorrect: it identifies **Increased acidity of oceans as a result of increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Increased plant growth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere**.
- **D: Adaptation of all living beings on Earth to the climate change brought about by the increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere** — Incorrect: it identifies **Adaptation of all living beings on Earth to the climate change brought about by the increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Increased plant growth due to increased concentration of carbon dioxide in the atmosphere**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**carbon fertilization effect on plant growth and atmosphere**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

**Secondary owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/25_Computing-Fundamentals-Hardware-Software-Networks-and-Cloud](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/25_Computing-Fundamentals-Hardware-Software-Networks-and-Cloud.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-internet-of-things-smart-connected-devices-scenario-description`

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

When the alarm of your smartphone rings in the morning, you wake up and tap it to stop the alarm which causes your geyser to be switched on automatically. The smart mirror in your bathroom shows the day's weather and also indicates the level of water in your overhead tank. After you take some groceries from your refrigerator for making breakfast, it recognises the shortage of stock in it and places an order for the supply of fresh grocery items. When you step out of your house and lock the door,

(a) Border Gateway Protocol  
(b) Internet of Things  
(c) Internet Protocol  
(d) Virtual Private Network  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Internet of Things**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The scenario vividly describes a network of interconnected physical objects, a smartphone, geyser, smart mirror, refrigerator, lights, fans, AC machines, and a car, all embedded with sensors, software, and other technologies for the purpose of connecting and exchanging data with other devices and systems over the internet. This seamless communication and automation of tasks, without human intervention for every single action, is the defining characteristic of the Internet of Things (IoT). Option A, Border Gateway Protocol (BGP), is a standardized exterior gateway protocol designed to exchange routing and reachability information between autonomous systems on the internet. It has no direct relevance to the interaction and automation of consumer devices described.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Border Gateway Protocol** — Incorrect: it identifies **Border Gateway Protocol**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Internet of Things**.
- **B: Internet of Things** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: Internet Protocol** — Incorrect: it identifies **Internet Protocol**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Internet of Things**.
- **D: Virtual Private Network** — Incorrect: it identifies **Virtual Private Network**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Internet of Things**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**internet of things smart connected devices scenario description**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to solar power production in India, consider the following statements:
1. India is the third largest in the world in the manufacture of silicon wafers used in photovoltaic units.
2. The solar power tariffs are determined by the Solar Energy Corporation of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. India is not the third largest in the world in the manufacture of silicon wafers used in photovoltaic units. The global silicon wafer manufacturing landscape is heavily dominated by China, which accounts for over 90% of the world's solar wafer manufacturing capacity. India has historically had very limited or no domestic manufacturing capacity for solar wafers and polysilicon ingots, relying heavily on imports. While India is making efforts to boost its domestic solar manufacturing ecosystem through policies like the Production Linked Incentive (PLI) scheme, it is still far from being among the top manufacturers of silicon wafers globally.

**Statement/pair-level result:**

- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

## Q68. East India Company export commodities from Bengal mid-century
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Economic Impact of British Rule](../../topics/modern-history/economic-impact-of-british-rule.md) → East India Company export commodities from Bengal mid-century
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Economic Impact of British Rule → East India Company export commodities from Bengal mid-century  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q68 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/07_Economic-Impact-of-British-Rule.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-east-india-company-export-commodities-from-bengal-mid-century`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The staple commodities of export by the English East India Company from Bengal in the middle of the 18th century were

(a) Raw cotton, oil-seeds and opium  
(b) Sugar, salt, zinc and lead  
(c) Copper, silver, gold, spices and tea  
(d) Cotton, silk, saltpetre and opium  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Cotton, silk, saltpetre and opium**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

During the middle of the 18th century, particularly after the Battle of Plassey (1757) and Buxar (1764) which gave the English East India Company significant control over Bengal, the Company actively pursued a policy of extracting resources for export to Britain and other markets. The primary objective was to acquire goods that commanded high prices in European markets. Option D is the most accurate representation of the staple exports from Bengal during this period. Cotton (primarily cotton textiles, rather than just raw cotton, as Bengal was a major weaving center) was a highly sought-after commodity in Europe. Silk, both raw and finished textiles, also constituted a significant portion of the Company's exports. Saltpetre, a crucial ingredient for gunpowder, was of immense strategic importance to European powers, especially given the frequent wars. Bengal was a major source of saltpetre. Opium, produced in Bengal, increasingly became a key export, particularly for the Company's trade with China to finance the purchase of tea.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Raw cotton, oil-seeds and opium** — Incorrect: it identifies **Raw cotton, oil-seeds and opium**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Cotton, silk, saltpetre and opium**.
- **B: Sugar, salt, zinc and lead** — Incorrect: it identifies **Sugar, salt, zinc and lead**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Cotton, silk, saltpetre and opium**.
- **C: Copper, silver, gold, spices and tea** — Incorrect: it identifies **Copper, silver, gold, spices and tea**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Cotton, silk, saltpetre and opium**.
- **D: Cotton, silk, saltpetre and opium** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**east india company export commodities from bengal mid-century**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q69"></a>
## Q69. Champaran Satyagraha significance for Indian National Movement
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Gandhian Movements and Mass Mobilisation](../../topics/modern-history/gandhian-mass-mobilisation.md) → Champaran Satyagraha significance for Indian National Movement
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Gandhis Rise Rowlatt and Jallianwala → Champaran Satyagraha significance for Indian National Movement  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q69 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: single-best answer · factual discrimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/19_Gandhis-Rise-Rowlatt-and-Jallianwala](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/19_Gandhis-Rise-Rowlatt-and-Jallianwala.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-champaran-satyagraha-significance-for-indian-national-movement`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following is a very significant aspect of the Champaran Satyagraha?

(a) Active all-India participation of lawyers, students and women in the National Movement  
(b) Active involvement of Dalit and Tribal communities of India in the National Movement  
(c) Joining of peasant unrest to India's National Movement  
(d) Drastic decrease in the cultivation of plantation crops and commercial crops  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Joining of peasant unrest to India's National Movement**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Champaran Satyagraha (1917) was Mahatma Gandhi's first major satyagraha in India and focused on the plight of indigo cultivators in Champaran, Bihar. Under the 'tinkathia' system, peasants were forced to cultivate indigo on 3/20th of their land and sell it at prices fixed by European planters, leading to immense exploitation. When Gandhi intervened, he transformed a local peasant grievance into a struggle with national implications. Option C is the most significant aspect because the Champaran Satyagraha successfully brought the long-standing, localized peasant unrest against exploitative British planters into the fold of the broader Indian National Movement. Gandhi's methods of non-violent civil disobedience demonstrated how local issues could be effectively addressed through mass mobilization and linked to the larger struggle for self-rule. This marked a new phase where the national movement began to address the specific economic grievances of the masses, particularly peasants.

**Option-by-option check:**

With reference to the religious practices in India, the "Sthanakvasi" sect belongs to

(a) Buddhism  
(b) Jainism  
(c) Vaishnavism  
(d) Shaivism  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Jainism**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The 'Sthanakvasi' sect is a sub-sect within Jainism, specifically belonging to the Shvetambara tradition. This sect emerged around the 17th century AD, founded by a merchant named Lavaji. A key distinguishing feature of the Sthanakvasi is their rejection of idol worship, setting them apart from other Jain sub-sects. Instead of worshipping idols in temples, Sthanakvasis perform their religious practices in designated halls or meditation centers, known as 'Sthanaks'. This question tests direct knowledge of various religious traditions and their sub-sects, which is a recurring theme in the 'Art & Culture' section of the UPSC CSE Prelims. The other options, Buddhism, Vaishnavism, and Shaivism, are distinct major Indian religions or Hindu denominations and do not include the Sthanakvasi sect.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Buddhism** — Incorrect: it identifies **Buddhism**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Jainism**.

- **C: Vaishnavism** — Incorrect: it identifies **Vaishnavism**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Jainism**.
- **D: Shaivism** — Incorrect: it identifies **Shaivism**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Jainism**.

1. White marble was used in making Buland Darwaza and Khankah at Fatehpur Sikri.

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — Neither 1 nor 2**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The Buland Darwaza at Fatehpur Sikri, built by Emperor Akbar, is predominantly constructed from red and buff sandstone. Fatehpur Sikri's architecture, in general, is characterized by the extensive use of red sandstone. Similarly, the various 'Khankahs' or religious hospices within Fatehpur Sikri were also primarily built using red sandstone, consistent with the architectural style of the complex.

## Q73. Foreign traveler who discussed diamonds and diamond mines India

**Canonical slug:** `medieval-history-foreign-traveler-who-discussed-diamonds-and-diamond-mines-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following foreign travellers elaborately discussed about diamonds and diamond mines of India?

(a) Francois Bernier  
(b) Jean-Baptiste Tavernier  
(c) Jean de Thevenot  
(d) Abbe Barthelemy Carre  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Jean-Baptiste Tavernier**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Jean-Baptiste Tavernier was a renowned 17th-century French gem merchant and traveller, famous for his extensive six voyages to Persia and India between 1630 and 1668. His primary interest in India was commercial, particularly the trade of precious stones. His seminal work, 'Les Six Voyages de Jean-Baptiste Tavernier' (Six Voyages, 1675), provides detailed accounts of India's diamond mines, especially those in the Golconda region, and the processes of extraction, cutting, and valuation of diamonds. He not only described these mines but also actively participated in the diamond trade, handling some of history's most famous diamonds, including the 'Tavernier Blue' which later became the Hope Diamond. This meticulous documentation makes him the most relevant traveller among the options who 'elaborately discussed' diamonds and diamond mines. Francois Bernier, also a French traveller, was a physician and philosopher known for his 'Travels in the Mughal Empire' (1656-1668), which primarily focused on the socio-political conditions and the Mughal court, not specifically diamonds. Jean de Thevenot was another French traveller who wrote about his journeys to the Levant, Persia, and India, detailing routes and customs, but his accounts were not as focused on the diamond trade as Tavernier's. Abbe Barthelemy Carre's travels in India and the Near East (1672-1674) provided insights into the French East India Company's activities and general observations, but again, not a detailed discourse on diamonds.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Francois Bernier** — Incorrect: it identifies **Francois Bernier**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Jean-Baptiste Tavernier**.
- **B: Jean-Baptiste Tavernier** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: Jean de Thevenot** — Incorrect: it identifies **Jean de Thevenot**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Jean-Baptiste Tavernier**.
- **D: Abbe Barthelemy Carre** — Incorrect: it identifies **Abbe Barthelemy Carre**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Jean-Baptiste Tavernier**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**foreign traveler who discussed diamonds and diamond mines india**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q74. Maitreya as future Buddha in Buddhist tradition
**Primary route:** Ancient History → [Jainism and Buddhism](../../topics/ancient-history/jainism-and-buddhism.md) → Maitreya as future Buddha in Buddhist tradition
**Primary classification:** Ancient History → Jainism and Buddhism → Maitreya as future Buddha in Buddhist tradition

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Indian history, who among the following is a future Buddha, yet to come to save the world?

(a) Avalokiteshvara  
(b) Lokesvara  
(c) Maitreya  
(d) Padmapani  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Maitreya**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The question asks to identify the future Buddha, who is yet to appear to save the world, in the context of Indian history. In Buddhist eschatology, Maitreya is recognized as the future Buddha. According to Buddhist scriptures, Maitreya will be the successor to the present Buddha, Gautama Buddha (Shakyamuni Buddha). He is prophesied to appear on Earth in the distant future, achieve complete enlightenment, and teach the pure dharma. This is a Bodhisattva who embodies the compassion of all Buddhas. He is widely revered in Mahayana Buddhism and is known for his vow to postpone his own Buddhahood until all sentient beings are liberated from samsara. He is a present Bodhisattva, not a future Buddha. This is another name or an epithet for Avalokiteshvara, especially popular in Southeast Asian Buddhist traditions. like Avalokiteshvara, he is a Bodhisattva of compassion, not the future Buddha.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Avalokiteshvara** — Incorrect: it identifies **Avalokiteshvara**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Maitreya**.
- **B: Lokesvara** — Incorrect: it identifies **Lokesvara**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Maitreya**.
- **C: Maitreya** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: Padmapani** — Incorrect: it identifies **Padmapani**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Maitreya**.

## Q75. Subsidiary Alliance introduced by Lord Wellesley provisions

**Primary classification:** Modern History → British Territorial Expansion → Subsidiary Alliance introduced by Lord Wellesley provisions

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following statements does not apply to the system of Subsidiary Alliance introduced by Lord Wellesley?

(a) To maintain a large standing army at other's expense  
(b) To keep India safe from Napoleonic danger  
(c) To secure a fixed income for the Company  
(d) To establish British paramountcy over the Indian States  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — To secure a fixed income for the Company**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Subsidiary Alliance system, introduced by Lord Wellesley (Governor-General from 1798-1805), was a key instrument of British imperial expansion and control in India. Let's analyze each option: Option A: 'To maintain a large standing army at other's expense' - This statement is correct and applies to the Subsidiary Alliance. Under this system, Indian rulers had to agree to the permanent stationing of a British force within their territory and to pay for its maintenance. If they failed to make the payment, a portion of their territory was ceded to the British. This effectively allowed the British to maintain a large army without bearing its full financial burden, funded by the Indian states. Option B: 'To keep India safe from Napoleonic danger'

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: To maintain a large standing army at other's expense** — Incorrect: it identifies **To maintain a large standing army at other's expense**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **To secure a fixed income for the Company**.
- **B: To keep India safe from Napoleonic danger** — Incorrect: it identifies **To keep India safe from Napoleonic danger**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **To secure a fixed income for the Company**.
- **C: To secure a fixed income for the Company** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: To establish British paramountcy over the Indian States** — Incorrect: it identifies **To establish British paramountcy over the Indian States**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **To secure a fixed income for the Company**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**subsidiary alliance introduced by lord wellesley provisions**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q76"></a>
## Q76. Introduction of English education in colonial India factors
**Primary route:** Modern History → [Macaulay’s Minute and Colonial Education](../../topics/modern-history/macaulays-minute-colonial-education.md) → Introduction of English education in colonial India factors
**Primary classification:** Modern History → Social and Cultural Policy Education Press → Introduction of English education in colonial India factors  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q76 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/09_Social-and-Cultural-Policy-Education-Press](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/09_Social-and-Cultural-Policy-Education-Press.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-introduction-of-english-education-in-colonial-india-factors`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Charter Act of 1813
2. General Committee of Public Instruction, 1823
3. Orientalist and Anglicist Controversy
Select the correct answer using the code given below: Which of the following led to the introduction of English Education in India?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2 and 3**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

All three statements are correct and collectively led to the introduction of English Education in India. Charter Act of 1813: This Act for the first time made a financial provision for education in India. It mandated the East India Company to spend one lakh rupees annually 'for the revival and improvement of literature and the encouragement of the learned natives of India, and for the introduction and promotion of a knowledge of the sciences among the inhabitants of the British territories in India.' While it didn't explicitly state 'English education,' it initiated the funding and the debate over the medium and content of education. General Committee of Public Instruction, 1823: Formed to manage the one lakh rupees sanctioned by the Charter Act of 1813, this committee was responsible for allocating funds for education. It was within this committee that the great debate on the nature of education and medium of instruction, whether it should be Oriental (Indian languages, traditional knowledge) or Occidental (Western sciences, English medium), took place, ultimately leading to the Anglicist victory. Orientalist and Anglicist Controversy: This was a significant intellectual debate from the 1820s to the 1830s among British officials and Indian intellectuals regarding the direction of education. The Orientalists, led by figures like H. T. Prinsep and H.H. Wilson, advocated for the promotion of Indian classical languages (Sanskrit, Persian, Arabic) and traditional learning. The Anglicists, championed by Macaulay and Charles Trevelyan, argued for the promotion of Western sciences and literature through the medium of English. Macaulay's 'Minute on Indian Education' (1835) strongly sided with the Anglicists, leading to Lord William Bentinck's resolution that officially adopted English as the medium of instruction for higher education and promoted Western learning. this controversy directly shaped the policy outcome.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 1, 3.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**introduction of english education in colonial india factors**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which one of the following is an artificial lake?

(a) Kodaikanal (Tamil Nadu)  
(b) Kolleru (Andhra Pradesh)  
(c) Nainital (Uttarakhand)  
(d) Renuka (Himachal Pradesh)  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Kodaikanal (Tamil Nadu)**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Kodaikanal Lake, located in Kodaikanal, Tamil Nadu, is an artificial, star-shaped lake built in 1863 by Sir Vere Henry Levinge, the then Collector of Madurai. It was created by damming the outlet of two streams, primarily for recreational purposes. Kolleru Lake (Andhra Pradesh) is one of the largest freshwater natural lakes in India, situated between the Godavari and Krishna deltas. It is a natural flood-balancing reservoir and a Ramsar site. Nainital Lake (Uttarakhand) is a natural freshwater lake, crescent-shaped, and is tectonically formed in the Kumaon Himalayas. Renuka Lake (Himachal Pradesh) is the largest natural lake in Himachal Pradesh, located in the Sirmour district. It is also a Ramsar site and is considered sacred.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Kodaikanal (Tamil Nadu)** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: Kolleru (Andhra Pradesh)** — Incorrect: it identifies **Kolleru (Andhra Pradesh)**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Kodaikanal (Tamil Nadu)**.
- **C: Nainital (Uttarakhand)** — Incorrect: it identifies **Nainital (Uttarakhand)**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Kodaikanal (Tamil Nadu)**.
- **D: Renuka (Himachal Pradesh)** — Incorrect: it identifies **Renuka (Himachal Pradesh)**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Kodaikanal (Tamil Nadu)**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**artificial lake identification among given indian water bodies**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q78. Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana scope provisions and ministry
**Primary route:** Economy → [Employment, Labour Codes, Skills and Demographic Dividend](../../topics/economy/employment-labour-codes-skills-and-demographic-dividend.md) → Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana scope provisions and ministry

**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/22_Employment-Labour-Codes-Skills-and-Demographic-Dividend.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-pradhan-mantri-kaushal-vikas-yojana-scope-provisions-and-ministry`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana, consider the following statements:
1. It is the flagship scheme of the Ministry of Labour and Employment.
2. It, among other things, will also impart training in soft skills, entrepreneurship, financial and digital literacy.
3. It aims to align the competencies of the unregulated workforce of the country to the National Skill Qualification Framework.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) 2 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The Pradhan Mantri Kaushal Vikas Yojana (PMKVY) is the flagship scheme of the Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship (MSDE), not the Ministry of Labour and Employment. The MSDE was created specifically to focus on the skill development ecosystem in India, and PMKVY is a core initiative under this ministry. Statement 2 is correct. PMKVY’s Short Term Training component explicitly states that, in addition to providing training aligned with the National Skills Qualification Framework (NSQF), Training Centres (TCs) shall also impart training in essential life skills such as Soft Skills, Entrepreneurship, Financial Literacy, and Digital Literacy. This holistic approach aims to make trainees more employable and well-rounded.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **B: 2 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **C: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**pradhan mantri kaushal vikas yojana scope provisions and ministry**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q79. All India Home Rule League renamed Swarajya Sabha 1920
**Primary route:** Modern History → [First World War, Home Rule League & the Lucknow Pact](../../topics/modern-history/wwi-home-rule-and-lucknow-pact.md) → All India Home Rule League renamed Swarajya Sabha 1920
**Primary classification:** Modern History → WWI Home Rule and Lucknow Pact → All India Home Rule League renamed Swarajya Sabha 1920  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q79 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Modern-Indian-History/basic/18_WWI-Home-Rule-and-Lucknow-Pact](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Modern-Indian-History/basic/18_WWI-Home-Rule-and-Lucknow-Pact.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `modern-history-all-india-home-rule-league-renamed-swarajya-sabha-1920`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In 1920, which of the following changed its name to "Swarajya Sabha"?

(a) All India Home Rule League  
(b) Hindu Mahasabha  
(c) South Indian Liberal Federation  
(d) The Servants of India Society  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — All India Home Rule League**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is (A) All India Home Rule League. In 1920, Mahatma Gandhi was elected as the president of the All India Home Rule League. Under his leadership, the organization changed its name to 'Swarajya Sabha' to better reflect the evolving goals of the Indian nationalist movement, which were moving beyond the demand for 'Home Rule' (self-government within the British Empire) towards 'Swaraj' (complete self-rule or independence). This change also aligned the organization's name with the spirit of the Non-Cooperation Movement, which Gandhi was spearheading. The other options are incorrect: (B) Hindu Mahasabha was a Hindu nationalist organization formed in 1915, primarily focused on protecting Hindu interests, and did not change its name to Swarajya Sabha. (C) South Indian Liberal Federation, popularly known as the Justice Party, was founded in 1916 and focused on non-Brahmin rights and representation in Madras Presidency; it also did not undergo this name change.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: All India Home Rule League** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: Hindu Mahasabha** — Incorrect: it identifies **Hindu Mahasabha**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **All India Home Rule League**.
- **C: South Indian Liberal Federation** — Incorrect: it identifies **South Indian Liberal Federation**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **All India Home Rule League**.
- **D: The Servants of India Society** — Incorrect: it identifies **The Servants of India Society**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **All India Home Rule League**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**all india home rule league renamed swarajya sabha 1920**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q80. Chronological order of 19th century socio-religious reform events

**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Which among the following events happened earliest ?

(a) Swami Dayanand established Arya Samaj.  
(b) Dinabandhu Mitra wrote *Neeldarpan*.  
(c) Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay wrote *Anandmath*.  
(d) Satyendranath Tagore became the first Indian to succeed in the Indian Civil Services Examination.  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — Dinabandhu Mitra wrote *Neeldarpan*.**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

To determine the earliest event, we need to establish the dates for each option: Option A: Swami Dayanand established Arya Samaj. Arya Samaj was founded by Swami Dayanand Saraswati in Bombay (now Mumbai) on April 10, 1875. Option B: Dinabandhu Mitra wrote (The Mirror of Indigo) between 1858 and 1859, and it was published in 1860. Option C: Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay wrote is a Bengali historical novel written by Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay and was published in 1882. Option D: Satyendranath Tagore became the first Indian to succeed in the Indian Civil Services Examination. Satyendranath Tagore was the first Indian to qualify for the Indian Civil Service (ICS) examination in 1863. He joined the service in 1864. Comparing the dates, Dinabandhu Mitra's (1858-1859/1860) is the earliest event among the given options. Option B is the correct answer.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Swami Dayanand established Arya Samaj.** — Incorrect: it identifies **Swami Dayanand established Arya Samaj.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Dinabandhu Mitra wrote *Neeldarpan*.**.
- **B: Dinabandhu Mitra wrote *Neeldarpan*.** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay wrote *Anandmath*.** — Incorrect: it identifies **Bankim Chandra Chattopadhyay wrote *Anandmath*.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Dinabandhu Mitra wrote *Neeldarpan*.**.
- **D: Satyendranath Tagore became the first Indian to succeed in the Indian Civil Services Examination.** — Incorrect: it identifies **Satyendranath Tagore became the first Indian to succeed in the Indian Civil Services Examination.**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Dinabandhu Mitra wrote *Neeldarpan*.**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**chronological order of 19th century socio-religious reform events**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q81"></a>
## Q81. Heavy sand mining riverbeds environmental and groundwater consequences
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Water Pollution and River Cleaning Missions](../../topics/environment/water-pollution-and-river-cleaning-missions.md) → Heavy sand mining riverbeds environmental and groundwater consequences
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Water Pollution and River Cleaning Missions → Heavy sand mining riverbeds environmental and groundwater consequences  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q81 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/14_Water-Pollution-and-River-Cleaning-Missions](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/14_Water-Pollution-and-River-Cleaning-Missions.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-heavy-sand-mining-riverbeds-environmental-and-groundwater-consequences`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Decreased salinity in the river
2. Pollution of groundwater
3. Lowering of the water-table
Select the correct answer using the code given below: Which of the following is/are the possible consequence/s of heavy sand mining in riverbeds?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Decreased salinity in the river: This statement is incorrect. Heavy sand mining, especially in coastal or estuarine areas, can lead to increased saltwater intrusion into the river and adjacent aquifers, thereby increasing salinity, not decreasing it. In non-coastal areas, sand mining primarily affects sediment transport, river morphology, and groundwater interaction, and does not directly cause a decrease in salinity. Pollution of groundwater: This statement is correct. Riverbeds, composed of sand and gravel, act as natural filters for water seeping into the groundwater system. Excessive sand mining removes this natural filter, making the groundwater more vulnerable to contamination from surface pollutants (e.g., agricultural runoff, industrial discharge, sewage) in the river or from the land. Additionally, the machinery used in mining can itself introduce pollutants. Lowering of the water-table: This statement is correct. Sand and gravel in the riverbed are crucial for groundwater recharge and maintaining the water table in adjacent areas. When large quantities of sand are removed, the river channel deepens. This lowering of the riverbed effectively lowers the local base level for the surrounding groundwater, causing the water table to drop. This can lead to wells drying up and increased pumping costs. statements 2 and 3 are correct consequences of heavy sand mining.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1; omits supported statement(s) 2, 3.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**heavy sand mining riverbeds environmental and groundwater consequences**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.

## Q82. Agricultural soil organic matter sulfur cycle and salinization
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Desertification, UNCCD and Land Degradation](../../topics/environment/desertification-unccd-and-land-degradation.md) → Agricultural soil organic matter sulfur cycle and salinization
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Desertification UNCCD and Land Degradation → Agricultural soil organic matter sulfur cycle and salinization  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q82 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/23_Desertification-UNCCD-and-Land-Degradation](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/23_Desertification-UNCCD-and-Land-Degradation.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-agricultural-soil-organic-matter-sulfur-cycle-and-salinization`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to agricultural soils, consider the following statements:
1. A high content of organic matter in soil drastically reduces its water holding capacity.
2. Soil does not play any role in the sulphur cycle.
3. Irrigation over a period of time can contribute to the salinization of some agricultural lands.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 3 only  
(c) 1 and 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

This statement is incorrect. Organic matter acts like a sponge, significantly the soil's water holding capacity. It improves soil structure, aeration, and water infiltration, allowing soil to retain more moisture for plant use. A higher organic matter content is beneficial for drought resistance and overall soil health. This statement is incorrect. Soil plays a crucial and indispensable role in the sulphur cycle. Sulphur exists in various forms in the soil, both organic and inorganic. Soil microorganisms (bacteria and fungi) are vital for the transformation of sulphur compounds, including the mineralization of organic sulphur, oxidation of sulphides to sulphates, and reduction of sulphates. Plants absorb sulphur primarily in the sulphate form from the soil. soil is a central reservoir and an active site for sulphur transformations.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 2; omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **B: 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 1 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 2.

## Q83. Partnership for Action on Green Economy PAGE UN mechanism
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [UNFCCC COP Kyoto Paris Agreement](../../topics/environment/unfccc-cop-kyoto-paris-agreement.md) → Partnership for Action on Green Economy PAGE UN mechanism
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → UNFCCC COP Kyoto Paris Agreement → Partnership for Action on Green Economy PAGE UN mechanism

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

The Partnership for Action on Green Economy (PAGE), a UN mechanism to assist countries transition towards greener and more inclusive economies, emerged at

(a) The Earth Summit on Sustainable Development 2002, Johannesburg  
(b) The United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development 2012, Rio de Janeiro  
(c) The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change 2015, Paris  
(d) The World Sustainable Development Summit 2016, New Delhi  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — The United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development 2012, Rio de Janeiro**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Partnership for Action on Green Economy (PAGE) was launched in 2012 at the United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development (UNCSD), also known as Rio+20, held in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil. This conference focused on two primary themes: a green economy in the context of sustainable development and poverty eradication, and the institutional framework for sustainable development. PAGE emerged as a direct response to the call at Rio+20 for a transition to greener and more inclusive economies. Option A, The Earth Summit on Sustainable Development 2002, Johannesburg (also known as the World Summit on Sustainable Development - WSSD), aimed to review progress since the 1992 Earth Summit and focused on implementation, particularly addressing poverty and environmental degradation. PAGE did not emerge here.

**Option-by-option check:**
