- **B: The United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development 2012, Rio de Janeiro** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change 2015, Paris** — Incorrect: it identifies **The United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change 2015, Paris**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development 2012, Rio de Janeiro**.
- **D: The World Sustainable Development Summit 2016, New Delhi** — Incorrect: it identifies **The World Sustainable Development Summit 2016, New Delhi**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **The United Nations Conference on Sustainable Development 2012, Rio de Janeiro**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**partnership for action on green economy page un mechanism**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q84"></a>
## Q84. 3D printing technology applications across multiple sectors
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Nanotechnology and Applications](../../topics/science-and-technology/nanotechnology-and-applications.md) → 3D printing technology applications across multiple sectors
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Nanotechnology and Applications → 3D printing technology applications across multiple sectors  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q84 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/16_Nanotechnology-and-Applications](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/16_Nanotechnology-and-Applications.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-3d-printing-technology-applications-across-multiple-sectors`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

1. Preparation of confectionery items
2. Manufacture of bionic ears
3. Automotive industry
4. Reconstructive surgeries
5. Data processing technologies
Select the correct answer using the code given below: "3D printing" has applications in which of the following?

(a) 1, 3 and 4 only  
(b) 2, 3 and 5 only  
(c) 1 and 4 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's evaluate each statement regarding the applications of 3D printing: Preparation of confectionery items: True. 3D food printing is a rapidly developing field that allows for the creation of intricate and customized designs in confectionery items such as chocolates, cakes, and other edible decorations. It enables high precision and personalization in food preparation. Manufacture of bionic ears: True. 3D printing is extensively used in the medical and healthcare sector for creating custom prosthetics, including bionic ears, hearing aids, and other assistive devices. Bioprinting, a specialized form of 3D printing, is also actively researched for fabricating living tissues and organs. Automotive industry: True. The automotive sector widely employs 3D printing for various purposes, including rapid prototyping of new parts, manufacturing of complex and lightweight components, creating custom tooling, and even producing specialized end-use parts. This accelerates design cycles and reduces production costs. Reconstructive surgeries: True. 3D printing plays a critical role in reconstructive surgeries by enabling the creation of patient-specific implants, prosthetics, anatomical models for surgical planning, and custom surgical guides. This technology enhances precision, improves fit, and leads to better patient outcomes. Data processing technologies: True. While 3D printing is primarily a manufacturing process, its applications extend to supporting and enabling advancements in data processing technologies. For example, 3D printing is used for rapid prototyping of components for electronic devices, including microchips, circuit boards, and custom enclosures for servers, data centers, and specialized computing hardware. It can create optimized cooling solutions or unique form factors for hardware essential to data processing, thereby enabling more efficient and advanced data processing infrastructure. Since all five statements describe valid applications of 3D printing, the correct answer is D.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q85"></a>
## Q85. Barren Island active volcano location and eruption history
**Primary route:** Geography → [Vulcanism and Earthquakes](../../topics/geography/vulcanism-and-earthquakes.md) → Barren Island active volcano location and eruption history
**Primary classification:** Geography → Vulcanism and Earthquakes → Barren Island active volcano location and eruption history  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q85 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Geography/basic/03_Vulcanism-and-Earthquakes](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Geography/basic/03_Vulcanism-and-Earthquakes.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `geography-barren-island-active-volcano-location-and-eruption-history`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The Barren Island volcano is an active volcano located in the Indian territory.
2. Barren Island lies about 140 km east of Great Nicobar.
3. The last time the Barren Island volcano erupted was in 1991 and it has remained inactive since then.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. Barren Island is indeed India's only confirmed active volcano and is located in the Indian territory, specifically in the Andaman Sea, part of the Andaman and Nicobar Islands. Statement 2 is incorrect. Barren Island is located approximately 135-140 km northeast of Port Blair, the capital of Andaman and Nicobar Islands. Great Nicobar Island is situated much further south in the Nicobar group of islands, not directly east of Barren Island, and the distance mentioned is not accurate for its relation to Great Nicobar. Statement 3 is incorrect. The Barren Island volcano has not remained inactive since 1991. It has erupted multiple times after 1991, with significant activity reported in 2005-2006, 2017, 2020, and even as recently as January 2022 and May 2023. the statement that it has remained inactive since 1991 is factually wrong.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2, 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **D: 1 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.

(a) The Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change  
(b) The UNEP Secretariat  
(c) The UNFCCC Secretariat  
(d) The World Meteorological Organization  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — The UNFCCC Secretariat**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The "Momentum for Change: Climate Neutral Now" initiative was launched by the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) Secretariat. This initiative is designed to highlight and showcase the wide range of climate action being taken around the world by various stakeholders, including businesses, governments, communities, and individuals. It encourages all non-Party stakeholders to take a voluntary pledge to achieve climate neutrality by measuring their greenhouse gas emissions, reducing them as much as possible, and offsetting the remainder. The Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) is the leading international body for assessing climate change. Its primary role is to provide scientific assessments, not to launch public engagement initiatives or campaigns for climate neutrality.

This pair is correctly matched. Pair 2 is correct. Pair 3: Fort William College - Arthur Wellesley

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Right to Privacy is protected as an intrinsic part of Right to Life and Personal Liberty. Which of the following in the Constitution of India correctly and appropriately imply the above statement ?

(a) Article 14 and the provisions under the 42nd Amendment to the Constitution  
(b) Article 17 and the Directive Principles of State Policy in Part IV  
(c) Article 21 and the freedoms guaranteed in Part III  
(d) Article 24 and the provisions under the 44th Amendment to the Constitution  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — Article 21 and the freedoms guaranteed in Part III**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The correct answer is C. The Supreme Court of India, in its landmark judgment in K.S. Puttaswamy v. Union of India (2017), unanimously declared that the Right to Privacy is a fundamental right. The nine-judge bench held that privacy is an intrinsic part of the Right to Life and Personal Liberty guaranteed under Article 21 of the Constitution of India. While the right to privacy is primarily rooted in Article 21, it draws sustenance from and extends to other fundamental rights guaranteed in Part III of the Constitution, such as the freedom of speech and expression (Article 19(1)(a)), freedom to form associations (Article 19(1)(c)), and freedom of movement (Article 19(1)(d)), etc., as these freedoms often have a privacy dimension. Option A is incorrect. Article 14 deals with 'Equality before law' and 'Equal protection of laws' and does not directly encompass the Right to Privacy as an intrinsic part of life and personal liberty. The 42nd Amendment did not specifically deal with the Right to Privacy in this context.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Article 14 and the provisions under the 42nd Amendment to the Constitution** — Incorrect: it identifies **Article 14 and the provisions under the 42nd Amendment to the Constitution**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Article 21 and the freedoms guaranteed in Part III**.
- **B: Article 17 and the Directive Principles of State Policy in Part IV** — Incorrect: it identifies **Article 17 and the Directive Principles of State Policy in Part IV**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Article 21 and the freedoms guaranteed in Part III**.
- **C: Article 21 and the freedoms guaranteed in Part III** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: Article 24 and the provisions under the 44th Amendment to the Constitution** — Incorrect: it identifies **Article 24 and the provisions under the 44th Amendment to the Constitution**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Article 21 and the freedoms guaranteed in Part III**.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**right to privacy protected under article 21 and part iii**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.  
**Examiner trap:** Close names, reversed functions, absolute words, anachronism, and combination options can convert one doubtful proposition into a wrong answer.  
**Evidence anchor:** Polity/basic/Fundamental-Rights; held Series-A booklet Q92; held official-final Series-A key grid.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q93"></a>
## Q93. MSP for crops announced by Cabinet Committee Economic Affairs
**Primary route:** Economy → [MSP, Procurement, Buffer Stocks, PDS and Food Security](../../topics/economy/msp-procurement-buffer-stocks-pds-and-food-security.md) → MSP for crops announced by Cabinet Committee Economic Affairs
**Primary classification:** Economy → MSP Procurement Buffer Stocks PDS and Food Security → MSP for crops announced by Cabinet Committee Economic Affairs  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q93 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/12_MSP-Procurement-Buffer-Stocks-PDS-and-Food-Security](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/12_MSP-Procurement-Buffer-Stocks-PDS-and-Food-Security.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-msp-for-crops-announced-by-cabinet-committee-economic-affairs`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following:
1. Areca nut
2. Barley
3. Coffee
4. Finger millet
5. Groundnut 6. Sesamum 7. Turmeric The Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs has announced the Minimum Support Price for which of the above?

(a) 1, 2, 3 and 7 only  
(b) 2, 4, 5 and 6 only  
(c) 1, 3, 4, 5 and 6 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2, 4, 5 and 6 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

The Minimum Support Price (MSP) is a policy by the Government of India to safeguard farmers against a sharp fall in market prices. The Cabinet Committee on Economic Affairs (CCEA) announces the MSP for various crops based on the recommendations of the Commission for Agricultural Costs and Prices (CACP). Areca nut is primarily a plantation crop and is generally not included in the central government's list of crops for which MSP is announced. While some state governments might have specific procurement schemes, it is not covered by the CCEA's MSP for major agricultural commodities. Barley is a rabi crop and is one of the seven cereals for which MSP is announced by the CCEA. Coffee is a plantation crop and is typically not covered under the central government's MSP mechanism. Its prices are usually influenced by global market dynamics. Finger millet is one of the coarse grains (millets) for which MSP is announced. Groundnut is an oilseed and is included in the list of crops for which MSP is announced.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **5: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1, 2, 3 and 7 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 4, 5.
- **B: 2, 4, 5 and 6 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 1, 3, 4, 5 and 6 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3; omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 7** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1, 3.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**msp for crops announced by cabinet committee economic affairs**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Protected Area Network India](../../topics/environment/protected-area-network-india.md) → Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary state location in India
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Protected Area Network India → Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary state location in India

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

In which one of the following States is Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary located?

(a) Arunachal Pradesh  
(b) Manipur  
(c) Meghalaya  
(d) Nagaland  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — Arunachal Pradesh**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Pakhui Wildlife Sanctuary, also widely known as Pakke Wildlife Sanctuary or Pakke Tiger Reserve, is located in the East Kameng District of Arunachal Pradesh, in Northeast India. It is situated at the foothills of the Eastern Himalayas and is renowned for its rich biodiversity. The sanctuary was designated as a Tiger Reserve under Project Tiger in 2002. The other options are incorrect: Manipur is home to protected areas such as Keibul Lamjao National Park (famous for its floating phumdis) and Yangoupokpi-Lokchao Wildlife Sanctuary. Meghalaya features significant protected areas like Nokrek National Park, Balpakram National Park, and Siju Wildlife Sanctuary. Nagaland's prominent wildlife sanctuaries include Fakim Wildlife Sanctuary, Ghosu Bird Sanctuary, and Pulie Badze Wildlife Sanctuary.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: Arunachal Pradesh** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: Manipur** — Incorrect: it identifies **Manipur**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Arunachal Pradesh**.
- **C: Meghalaya** — Incorrect: it identifies **Meghalaya**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Arunachal Pradesh**.
- **D: Nagaland** — Incorrect: it identifies **Nagaland**, whereas the controlling fact/definition identifies **Arunachal Pradesh**.

## Q95. PSLV and GSLV satellite launch vehicles India comparison
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [Space Programme, ISRO, Launch Vehicles and Space Governance](../../topics/science-and-technology/space-programme-isro-launch-vehicles.md) → PSLV and GSLV satellite launch vehicles India comparison
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → Space Programme ISRO Launch Vehicles → PSLV and GSLV satellite launch vehicles India comparison  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q95 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/01_Space-Programme-ISRO-Launch-Vehicles](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/01_Space-Programme-ISRO-Launch-Vehicles.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-pslv-and-gslv-satellite-launch-vehicles-india-comparison`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to India's satellite launch vehicles, consider the following statements:
1. PSLVs launch the satellites useful for Earth resources monitoring whereas GSLVs are designed mainly to launch communication satellites.
2. Satellites launched by PSLV appear to remain permanently fixed in the same position in the sky, as viewed from a particular location on Earth.
3. GSLV Mk III is a four-staged launch vehicle with the first and third stages using solid rocket motors; and the second and fourth stages using liquid rocket engines.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3  
(c) 1 and 2  
(d) 3 only  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1: PSLVs launch the satellites useful for Earth resources monitoring whereas GSLVs are designed mainly to launch communication satellites. PSLV (Polar Satellite Launch Vehicle) is ISRO's workhorse for launching satellites into Polar Sun-Synchronous Orbits (SSO) and low Earth orbits (LEO). These orbits are ideal for remote sensing, Earth observation, meteorological satellites, and small scientific payloads, which are indeed useful for Earth resources monitoring. Examples include IRS (Indian Remote Sensing) series satellites. GSLV (Geosynchronous Satellite Launch Vehicle) is primarily designed to launch heavier communication satellites into Geostationary Transfer Orbit (GTO), from where they are maneuvered into the Geostationary Earth Orbit (GEO). Communication satellites require GEO to provide continuous coverage over a specific region on Earth. Statement 2: Satellites launched by PSLV appear to remain permanently fixed in the same position in the sky, as viewed from a particular location on Earth.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2, 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: 1 and 2** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 2.
- **D: 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**pslv and gslv satellite launch vehicles india comparison**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

## Q96. Public sector banking governance capital infusion and SBI merger
**Primary route:** Economy → [Banking Structure, NBFCs and Financial Regulation](../../topics/economy/banking-structure-nbfcs-and-financial-regulation.md) → Public sector banking governance capital infusion and SBI merger
**Primary classification:** Economy → Banking Structure NBFCs and Financial Regulation → Public sector banking governance capital infusion and SBI merger

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to the governance of public sector banking in India, consider the following statements:
1. Capital infusion into public sector banks by the Government of India has steadily increased in the last decade.
2. To put the public sector banks in order, the merger of associate banks with the parent State Bank of India has been affected.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 only  
(c) Both 1 and 2  
(d) Neither 1 nor 2  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. Capital infusion into Public Sector Banks (PSBs) by the Government of India has not steadily increased over the last decade. While there have been significant capital infusions, especially as part of recapitalization efforts to address Non-Performing Assets (NPAs) and meet Basel III norms, the trend has been fluctuating, not consistently increasing. For instance, large capital infusions occurred between FY17 and FY21, but there were also periods of reduced infusion or a shift towards recapitalization bonds rather than direct budgetary support, indicating a non-steady pattern. The term 'steadily increased' makes the statement false.

**Statement/pair-level result:**

- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q97"></a>
## Q97. Items exempted under Goods and Services Tax India
**Primary route:** Economy → [Taxation, GST, Finance Commission and Fiscal Federalism](../../topics/economy/taxation-gst-finance-commission-and-fiscal-federalism.md) → Items exempted under Goods and Services Tax India
**Primary classification:** Economy → Taxation GST Finance Commission and Fiscal Federalism → Items exempted under Goods and Services Tax India  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q97 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/10_Taxation-GST-Finance-Commission-and-Fiscal-Federalism.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-items-exempted-under-goods-and-services-tax-india`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following items:
1. Cereal grains hulled
2. Chicken eggs cooked
3. Fish processed and canned
4. Newspapers containing advertising material Which of the above items is/are exempted under GST (Goods and Services Tax)?

(a) 1 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 4 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (C) — 1, 2 and 4 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Let's analyze each item based on the GST exemption notifications by the Central Board of Indirect Taxes and Customs (CBIC): Cereal grains hulled: Unbranded food grains, including cereals, pulses, and flours (like atta, maida, besan), are exempted from GST. 'Hulled' grains, such as unpolished rice or split pulses (dal), when sold loose or unbranded, fall under this exemption. The intention behind this exemption is to keep essential food items affordable for the general public. While there have been changes post-July 2022 regarding pre-packaged and labelled food items, the question, typical of UPSC's general context for such items, refers to the broader exemption for basic, unbranded cereal grains. this item is exempted. Chicken eggs cooked: As per HSN 0407, 'Birds' eggs, in shell, fresh, preserved or cooked' are explicitly listed under the GST exemption notifications (Notification No. 2/2017-Central Tax (Rate), as amended). This means that even if eggs are cooked, as long as they are in their shell, they remain exempt from GST. This specifically applies to items like boiled eggs sold in their shell. this item is exempted. Fish processed and canned: While fresh fish is exempted from GST, processed, preserved, or canned fish products (e.g., canned tuna, smoked fish, fish fillets) are not. These items fall under different HSN codes (e.g., HSN 1604 for prepared or preserved fish) and are subject to various GST rates (typically 5%, 12%, or 18%) depending on the specific product and its preparation/packaging. this item is NOT exempted. Newspapers containing advertising material: Newspapers are explicitly exempted from GST. This exemption covers newspapers irrespective of whether they contain advertising material or not. The supply of newspapers, including supplements, falls under this exemption. this item is exempted. Based on the above analysis, items 1, 2, and 4 are exempted under GST. option C is the correct answer.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **4: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2, 4.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1, 4.
- **C: 1, 2 and 4 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **D: 1, 2, 3 and 4** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**items exempted under goods and services tax india**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q98"></a>
## Q98. Forest Rights Act Critical Wildlife Habitat and Baiga rights
**Primary route:** Environment and Ecology → [Forest Types and the Forest Rights Act](../../topics/environment/forest-types-and-forest-rights-act.md) → Forest Rights Act Critical Wildlife Habitat and Baiga rights
**Primary classification:** Environment and Ecology → Forest Types and Forest Rights Act → Forest Rights Act Critical Wildlife Habitat and Baiga rights  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q98 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Environment-and-Ecology/basic/11_Forest-Types-and-Forest-Rights-Act](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Environment-and-Ecology/basic/11_Forest-Types-and-Forest-Rights-Act.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `environment-and-ecology-forest-rights-act-critical-wildlife-habitat-and-baiga-rights`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following statements:
1. The definition of "Critical Wildlife Habitat" is incorporated in the Forest Rights Act, 2006.
2. For the first time in India, Baigas have been given Habitat Rights.
3. Union Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change officially decides and declares Habitat Rights for Primitive and Vulnerable Tribal Groups in any part of India.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (A) — 1 and 2 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is correct. The Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act, 2006, commonly known as the Forest Rights Act (FRA), defines "Critical Wildlife Habitat" (CWH) as 'such areas of National Parks and Wildlife Sanctuaries where it has been specifically established by the Ministry of Environment and Forests on the basis of scientific and objective criteria, that the co-existence of human activity is not possible and that these areas are required to be kept as inviolate for the purposes of wildlife conservation as may be determined and notified by the Central Government in the manner prescribed.' Statement 2 is correct. The Baiga Particularly Vulnerable Tribal Group (PVTG) residing in the Chhattisgarh state became the first community in India to be granted 'Habitat Rights' under the Forest Rights Act (FRA), 2006, in 2016. These rights were recognized for their traditional habitat in the buffer zone of Achanakmar Tiger Reserve.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3; omits supported statement(s) 1, 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 3.

<a id="2018-prelims-gs1-q99"></a>
## Q99. Vectors spreading plant diseases birds dust rain and wind
**Primary route:** Science and Technology → [General Science: Biology and Physiology](../../topics/science-and-technology/general-science-biology-and-physiology.md) → Vectors spreading plant diseases birds dust rain and wind
**Primary classification:** Science and Technology → General Science Biology and Physiology → Vectors spreading plant diseases birds dust rain and wind  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q99 · Difficulty: Hard · Demand: multiple-statement/application · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Science-and-Technology/basic/23_General-Science-Biology-and-Physiology.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `science-and-technology-vectors-spreading-plant-diseases-birds-dust-rain-and-wind`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

Consider the following:
1. Birds
2. Dust blowing
3. Rain
4. Wind blowing Which of the above spread plant diseases ?

(a) 1 and 3 only  
(b) 3 and 4 only  
(c) 1, 2 and 4 only  
(d) 1, 2, 3 and 4  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (D) — 1, 2, 3 and 4**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

All four options listed are known factors contributing to the spread of plant diseases. Birds can act as vectors for plant pathogens. They can carry fungal spores on their bodies, transport seeds of parasitic plants (like mistletoe), or even carry disease-infected insect vectors from one plant to another or from one location to another. For example, they can spread fungal diseases by depositing spores after feeding on infected plants. Dust particles, especially during strong winds, can carry microscopic pathogens such as fungal spores (e.g., rusts, mildews), bacterial cells, and even viral particles over considerable distances. These pathogens, once deposited on a susceptible plant, can initiate an infection.

- **4: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

## Q100. Organic farming NPOP APEDA and Sikkim first organic state
**Primary route:** Economy → [Land Reforms, Green Revolution and Cropping Systems](../../topics/economy/land-reforms-green-revolution-and-cropping-systems.md) → Organic farming NPOP APEDA and Sikkim first organic state
**Primary classification:** Economy → Land Reforms Green Revolution and Cropping Systems → Organic farming NPOP APEDA and Sikkim first organic state  
**Metadata:** 2018 · Prelims · GS-I · Series A · Official Q100 · Difficulty: Moderate · Demand: statement evaluation · elimination  
**Canonical owner:** [Economy/basic/11_Land-Reforms-Green-Revolution-and-Cropping-Systems](../../../upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/Economy/basic/11_Land-Reforms-Green-Revolution-and-Cropping-Systems.md)  
**Canonical slug:** `economy-organic-farming-npop-apeda-and-sikkim-first-organic-state`  
**Backlink:** [Paper-wise index](../../catalogue/PAPERWISE-INDEX.md)

### Official Series-A question (scan-reconciled transcription)

With reference to organic farming in India, consider the following statements:
1. 'The National Programme for Organic Production' (NPOP) is operated under the guidelines and directions of the Union Ministry of Rural Development.
2. "The Agricultural and Processed Food Products Export Development Authority' (APEDA) functions as the Secretariat for the implementation of NPOP.
3. Sikkim has become India's first fully organic State.
Which of the statements given above is/are correct?

(a) 1 and 2 only  
(b) 2 and 3 only  
(c) 3 only  
(d) 1, 2 and 3  

**Answer (UPSC official-final Series-A key): (B) — 2 and 3 only**

### Statement/option evaluation, elimination logic and trap

Statement 1 is incorrect. The National Programme for Organic Production (NPOP) is implemented by the Ministry of Commerce & Industry, Government of India, and not the Ministry of Rural Development. Its primary objective is to provide a mechanism for certification of organic products and to facilitate their export. Statement 2 is correct. The Agricultural and Processed Food Products Export Development Authority (APEDA), an organization under the Ministry of Commerce & Industry, functions as the Secretariat for the implementation of the NPOP. APEDA is responsible for the overall management and implementation of the programme, including accreditation of inspection and certification bodies.

**Statement/pair-level result:**
- **1: Incorrect.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **2: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.
- **3: Correct.** This status follows from the factual analysis above and the official keyed combination.

**Option-by-option check:**
- **A: 1 and 2 only** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1; omits supported statement(s) 3.
- **B: 2 and 3 only** — Correct: it matches the official-final Series-A key and the verified proposition/fact set.
- **C: 3 only** — Incorrect: omits supported statement(s) 2.
- **D: 1, 2 and 3** — Incorrect: includes rejected statement(s) 1.

**Elimination logic:** Fix the narrow core—**organic farming npop apeda and sikkim first organic state**—then test each qualifier, date, institution, location or statement before mapping the surviving set to A-D. Elimination supports evidence; it is not a substitute for it.
